[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:9:3)


**גַּם־זֶה הֶבֶל.** הוּא הַנִיתָּן לָרְשָׁעִים:


**This too is vanity.** It is what is given to the wicked.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 6:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:10:1)


**מַה־שֶּׁהָיָה כְּבָר נִקְרָא שְׁמוֹ.** חֲשִׁיבוֹת וּגְדֻלָּה שֶׁהָיְתָה לוֹ בְחַיָּיו, "כְּבָר נִקְרָא שְׁמוֹ", כְּלוֹמַר, כְּבָר הָיָה וְעָבַר יָצָא לוֹ שֵׁם בִּשְׂרָרָה, וְעַתָּה חָלַף, וְנוֹדָע שֶׁהוּא אָדָם וְלֹא אֵל, וְסוֹפוֹ שֶׁמֵּת, וְלֹא יוּכַל לָדוּן עִם מַלְאָךְ הַמָּוֶת שֶׁהוּא תַּקִּיף מִמֶּנּוּ:


**Whatever has been has already been named.** The stature and greatness that had been established during his lifetime, “has already been named,” i.e., it already was and passed, he already had attained fame, and now it has passed, and it was made known that he was a man¹¹ and not god, and his end was that he died, and he was unable to strive with the angel of death, who is more powerful than he.

footnotes:
¹¹ According to the Midrash, this refers to Adam. Note that the gematria (numerical value) of ‘מה’ [=45] is equal to ‘אדם’ [=45], man.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 6:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:11:1)


**כִּי יֵשׁ־דְּבָרִים הַרְבֵּה.** שֶׁנִּתְעַסֵּק בָּהֶן בְּחַיָּיו, כִּשְׂחוֹק הַמְּלָכִים, קֻפִּים וּפִילִים וַאֲרָיוֹת, הַכֹּל הִרְבָּה לוֹ וּמַה־יִוָתֵר לוֹ מִשֶּׁמֵת?:


**For there are many things.** With which he occupied himself during his lifetime, such as the games of the kings, [e.g.,] monkeys, elephants and lions. They increased vanity for him, and what remains for him after he dies.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 6:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:12:1)


**כִּי מִי־יוֹדֵעַ.** כִּי מִי אֲשֶׁר יוֹדֵעַ מַעֲשִׂים טוֹבִים, וּמַה לָּאָדָם לַעֲשׂוֹת בְּחַיָּיו, שֶׁיִּהְיוּ טוֹבִים לוֹ לָעוֹלָם הָאָרֹךְ:


**For who knows.** For who knows good deeds and what man should do during his life, so that it will bring him goodness in the hereafter.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 6:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:12:2)


**מִסְפַּר יְמֵי־חַיֵּי הֶבְלוֹ.** שֶׁהֵם מְעַט מִסְפָּר:


**In the counted days of his vanity.** Which are limited in number


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 6:12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:12:3)


**וְיַעֲשֵׂם.** לְאוֹתָם מַעֲשִׂים בְּשָׁעָה מֻעֶטֶת שֶׁהוּא חַי, שֶׁהִיא זְמַן קָצֵר כְּצֵל עוֹף הָעוֹבֵר, וְאַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁאָמַר שְׁלֹמֹה, "כַּצֵּל" סְתָם, וְלֹא פֵרַשׁ אִם צֵל דֶּקֶל אִם צֵל כּוֹתֶל, שֶׁהֵם קְבוּעִים, כְּבָר פֵּרְשׁוֹ דָוִד אָבִיו, "יָמָיו כְּצֵל עוֹבֵר", זֶהוּ צִלּוֹ שֶׁל עוֹף הַפּוֹרֵחַ, כַּךְ נִדְרָשׁ בַּמִּדְרָשׁ:


**Which he spends.** Those deeds, in the limited time that he lives, for this time is as short as the shadow of a passing bird,¹² and although Shlomo said, “like a shadow,” in general, and did not specify whether the shadow of a palm tree, or the shadow of a wall, which are permanent, his father Dovid had already explained it, “his days are like a passing shadow,”¹³ this is the shadow of a flying bird.¹⁴ It is thus expounded in this manner in the Midrash.

footnotes:
¹² Alternatively, “that he may do them [ויעשם] like a shadow,” i.e., while alive, a person should never rest, just as a shadow moves constantly as a result of the sun’s angle, so should a person constantly be on the move and be performing these good deeds constantly. (Metsudas Dovid)
¹³ Tehillim 144:4.
¹⁴ This is deduced in the following manner. A shadow has no permanency so when Scripture states “passing shadow,” it seems superfluous. Obviously, the “passing shadow” is a description of the object that is casting the shadow, e.g., a flying bird that passes [=flies] by.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 6:12:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:12:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/6:12:4)


**אֲשֶׁר מִי־יַגִיד לָאָדָם.** בַּמֶּה יִתְקַיֵּם הוֹן שֶׁקָּבַץ מֵעשֶׁק לְבָנָיו אַחֲרָיו תַּחַת הַשָּׁמֶשׁ:


**For who can tell a man.** How will the wealth that he gathered from oppression remain for his children after him, under the sun.



## Chapter 7


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:1:1)


**טוֹב שֵׁם מִשֶּׁמֶן טוֹב.** יָפֶה לְאָדָם שֵׁם טוֹב מִשֶּׁמֶן טוֹב. וּבְיוֹם הַמָּוֶת טוֹב הַשֵּׁם מִיּוֹם הִוָּלְדוֹ לְכַךְ הֻקַּשׁ שֵׁם טוֹב לְשֶׁמֶן יוֹתֵר מִשְּׁאָר מַשְׁקִין, שֶׁהַשֶּׁמֶן אַתָּה נוֹתֵן לְתוֹכוֹ מַיִם, וְהוּא צָף וְעוֹלֶה וְנִכָּר, אֲבָל שְׁאָר מַשְׁקִים, אַתָּה נוֹתֵן לְתוֹכָן מַיִם, וְהֵם בּוֹלְעִים.


**Better a good name than precious oil.** A fine reputation for a person is better than precious oil,¹ and on the day of [his] death that reputation is better than [it was] on the day he was born. For this reason, a good name is compared to oil in preference to other liquids, for [if] you put water into oil, it rises and floats, and is distinguishable, but other liquids, [if] you put water into them, it becomes absorbed.

footnotes:
¹ The Mishnah in Avos 4:13 states that there are three crowns, the crown of Torah, the crown of Kehunah and the crown of royalty, but the crown of a good name [כתר שם טוב], i.e., fine reputation, surpasses them all.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:1:2)


**טוֹב שֵׁם מִשֶּׁמֶן טוֹב.** שֶׁמֶן טוֹב יוֹרֵד לְמַטָּה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: "כַּשֶּׁמֶן הַטּוֹב . . . יוֹרֵד עַל הַזָּקָן". שֵׁם טוֹב עוֹלֶה לְמַעְלָה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: "וַאֲגַדְּלָה שְׁמֶךָ". שֶׁמֶן טוֹב לְשָׁעָה, וְשֵׁם טוֹב לְעוֹלָם, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: "יְהִי שְׁמוֹ לְעוֹלָם". שֶׁמֶן טוֹב הוֹלֵךְ מִקִּיטוֹן לִטְרַקְלִין וְלֹא יוֹתֵר, וְשֵׁם טוֹב לְסוֹף הָעוֹלָם. אָמַר רַבִּי יְהוּדָה בְּרַבִּי סִימוֹן: "מָצִינוּ בַּעֲלֵי שֶׁמֶן טוֹב נִכְנְסוּ לִמְקוֹם הַחַיִּים וְיָצְאוּ שְׂרוּפִים, וְהֵם נָדָב וַאֲבִיהוּא שֶׁנִּמְשְׁחוּ בְשֶׁמֶן הַמִּשְׁחָה, וּמָצִינוּ בַּעֲלֵי שֵׁם טוֹב שֶׁנִּכְנְסוּ לִמְקוֹם הַמִּיתָה וְיָצְאוּ חַיִּים, חֲנַנְיָה, מִישָׁאֵל וַעֲזַרְיָה, שֶׁיָּצְאוּ מִכִּבְשַׁן הָאֵשׁ:


**Better a good name than precious oil.** Precious oil runs down, as it is stated, “as the precious oil (on the head) runs down upon the beard.”² But a good name rises to the top, as it is stated, “and I will make your name great.”³ Precious oil is temporary, but a fine reputation is forever, as it is stated, “may his name endure forever.”⁴ Precious oil [at best] flows from the flask to the palace, and no further, but a fine reputation [spreads] throughout the world.⁵ Rabbi Yehudah the son of Rabbi Simon said, that we find that those who had good oil entered the place of life and emerged burnt up. These are Nadav and Avihu, who were anointed with the anointing oil. And we find those who possessed a fine reputation, who entered a place of death and emerged alive, [these are] Chananyah, Mishaeil, and Azaryah, who emerged [unscathed] from the fiery furnace.

footnotes:
² Tehillim 133:2.
³ Bereishis 12:2.
⁴ Tehillim 72:17.
⁵ The fragrance of good oil dissipates but a good name grows constantly stronger. (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:1:3)


**וְיוֹם הַמָּוֶת מִיּוֹם הִוָּלְדוֹ.** נוֹלְדָה מִרְיָם, אֵין הַכֹּל יוֹדְעִין מָה הִיא. מֵתָה, נִסְתַּלְּקָה הַבְּאֵר. וְכֵן אַהֲרֹן בְּעַמּוּד עָנָן, וְכֵן משֶׁה בְמָן:


**And the day of death than the day birth.** When Miriam was born, no one knew what she was. But when she died, the well ceased to exist, and similarly with Aharon with the pillar of cloud, and Moshe with the manna.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Alternatively, a fine reputation is better on the day of death than on the day of birth, because so long as one lives, his reputation is subject to possible future deterioration, whereas on the day of death there are no uncertainties. This thought is found in the Mishnah in Avos 2:4 wherein Hillel said, “do not believe in yourself until the day you die.” (Sforno) Or, it is good for one to consider his death [יום המות], beginning from the time that he is born [יום הולדו], and by doing so he will refrain from sinning. (Khilos Yaakov)



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:2:1)


**טוֹב לָלֶכֶת אֶל בֵּית אֵבֶל.** מִדָּה הַנּוֹהֶגֶת בַּחַיִּים וּבַמֵּתִים:


**Better to go to a house of mourning.** [Where] the type of conduct serves both the living and the dead.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:2:2)


**מִלֶּכֶת אֶל בֵּית מִשְׁתֶּה.** שֶׁהִיא מִדָּה שֶׁאֵינָהּ נוֹהֶגֶת אֶלָּא בַחַיִּים:


**Than to go to a house of feasting.** [Where] the type of conduct serves only the living.⁷ ⁸

footnotes:
⁷ Upon going to a house of mourning a person realizes that he too will be mourned when his time comes. This will cause him to be humbled and make him aware of sin. (Metsudas Dovid) Alternatively, the kindness done for the dead is absolute and the level of kindness [חסד של אמת], for the provider does anticipate any payback because that is “the end of all men.” However, when going to a house of feasting, e.g., a wedding, the newly married couple “take to heart” to remember that act of kindness so that they can return the kindness. The latter type of kindness is somewhat inferior to the former, and therefore Koheles says, “it is better to go to a house of mourning than to a house of feasting, etc. (Binah La’itim)
⁸ Frequenting the company of those who pursue honor and vanity arouses one’s lust and desire for those things, and that is the intent of this verse. (Mesilas Yeshorim)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:2:3)


**בַּאֲשֶׁר הוּא סוֹף כָּל הָאָדָם.** בִּשְׁבִיל שֶׁהָאֵבֶל הוּא סוֹף כָּל הָאָדָם. סוֹפוֹ שֶׁל כָּל אָדָם לָבֹא לִידֵי כָּךְ. לְפִיכָךְ, הַחַי יִתֵּן אֶל לִבּוֹ, כָּל מַה שֶּׁאֶגְמֹל חֶסֶד עִם הַמֵּת, אֶצְטָרֵךְ אֲנִי שֶׁיִּגְמְלוּ לִי בְמוֹתִי, דְּיִדַּל יִדְלוּנֵיהּ, דְּיִטְעַן יִטְעֲנוּנֵיהּ, דְיִסְפֹּד יִסְפְּדוּנֵיה, דִּילַוֶּה יְלַוִּנֵיהּ.


**For that is the end of all men.** Because mourning marks the end of every person. Every person will ultimately come to this. Therefore, “the living should take it to heart,” [to the fact that] whatever kindness I bestow upon the dead, I will need that they bestow [the same] upon me upon my death. He who raises his voice in lamentation, they will raise their voices in lamentation for him; he who bears the dead, they will bear him; he who eulogizes, they will eulogize him; he who escorts the dead, they will escort him.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:2:4)


דָּבָר אַחֵר: **בַּאֲשֶׁר הוּא סוֹף כָּל הָאָדָם.** שֶׁהַמִּיתָה הִיא סוֹף כָּל יְמֵי הָאָדָם, וְאִם לֹא עַכְשָׁיו גּוֹמֵל לוֹ חֶסֶד, לֹא יִגְמֹל לוֹ עוֹד, אֲבָל בֵּית הַמִּשְׁתֶּה, זִמְּנוֹ וְלֹא הָלַךְ, יוּכַל לוֹמַר לוֹ: "סוֹפָךְ שֶׁיִּוָּלֵד לְךָ בֵן, וְשָׁם אֶהְיֶה עִמָּךְ, תָּבֹא לָךְ שִׂמְחַת חֲתוּנַת בִּנְךָ וְשָׁם אֵלֵךְ:


Another explanation: **For that is the end of all men.** For death is the end of all man’s days, and if he does not bestow kindness upon him now, he will no longer [be able to] bestow it upon him, but [if] he invited him to a house of feasting and he did not go, he can say to him, “a son will ultimately be born to you, and I will be there with you, the joy of your child’s wedding will come to you, and I will go there.”


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:2:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:2:5)


**וְהַחַי יִתֵּן אֶל לִבּוֹ.** הַדָּבָר הַזֶּה, שֶׁאִם לֹא עַכְשָׁיו יִגְמֹל לוֹ חֶסֶד, לֹא יִגְמֹל לוֹ עוֹד:


**And the living should take it to heart.** This matter, that if he does not bestow kindness now, he will no longer [be able to] bestow it upon him.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Alternatively, if a person who is planning to do evil visits a house of mourning, he will surely “take it to heart” the matters dealing with death, and will thus abandon his plans and repent before God. (Targum)



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:3:1)


**טוֹב כַּעַס מִשְּׂחוֹק.** מִי שֶׁרוֹדֶפֶת מִדַּת הַדִּין אַחֲרָיו, אַל יִצְטַעֵר. טוֹב הָיָה לָהֶם לְדוֹר הַמַּבּוּל אִם הֶרְאָם הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא פָּנִים זְעוּמוֹת עַל עֲבֵרוֹת שֶׁבְּיָדָם, מִשְּׂחוֹק שֶׁשָּׂחַק עִמָּהֶם, שֶׁאִלּוּ הֶרְאָה לָהֶם קִימְעָא רוֹעַ פָּנִים, הָיוּ חוֹזְרִים לְמוּטָב. טוֹב הָיָה לַאֲדוֹנִיָּהוּ אִם עֲצָבוֹ אָבִיו עַל כָּל עֲבֵרָה שֶׁהָיָה עוֹשֶׂה, מִשְּׂחוֹק שֶׁהֶרְאָה לוֹ וּבְסוֹף נֶהֱרַג עָלָיו:


**Better vexation than levity.** If one is pursued by the [Divine] standard of justice, he should not be depressed.¹⁰ It would have been better for the generation of the flood if the Holy One, Blessed Is He, had showed them an angry countenance because of their sins, rather than the laughter that He laughed with them, for had He shown them a slight expression of displeasure, they would have returned to [do] good.¹¹ It would have been better for Adoniyahu had his father caused him grief for every sin that he committed, rather than the laughter that he showed him, and for which he was ultimately killed.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Alternatively, “better vexation stemming from levity,” i.e., when a situation calls for vexation [e.g., a teacher with his student], it should be only an appearance on the outside of anger, but not an emotional, true feeling, of anger. In the words of the Mesilas Yeshorim [Chapter 11] “any anger shown should be anger of the face and not anger of the heart.”
¹¹ See Maseches Shabbos 30b.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:3:2)


**יִיטַב לֵב.** יַהֲפֹךְ לֵב הָאָדָם לְהֵיטִיב דְּרָכָיו:


**The heart is made better.** It will reverse man’s heart and cause him to improve his ways.¹²

footnotes:
¹² Alternatively, as a result of the drought and retribution inflicted upon the world, the righteous will pray to God for mercy. (Targum)



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:4:1)


**לֵב חֲכָמִים בְּבֵית אֵבֶל.** מַחֲשַׁבְתָּם עַל יוֹם הַמִּיתָה:


**The heart of the wise [will reflect on] the house of mourning.** Their thoughts¹³) are [focused] on the day of death.¹⁴ ¹⁵

footnotes:
¹³ The literal translation is somewhat difficult because the heart of the wise is located in the same place as everyone else, therefore Rashi explains that it refers to their thoughts. (Sifsei Chachomim
¹⁴ These are their thoughts even when they are not visiting a house of mourning. As a result they subordinate their desires and avoid sin. (Metsudas Dovid)
¹⁵ Alternatively, the heart of the wise mourns over the destruction of the Beis Hamikdosh and grieve over Yisroel’s exile. (Targum)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:4:2)


**וְלֵב כְּסִילִים בְּבֵית שִׂמְחָה.** אֵין חֲרֵדִים מִיּוֹם הַמִּיתָה וְלִבָּם בָּרִיא כְאוּלָם:


**But the heart of the fools [are attracted] to the house of feasting.** They do not fear the[ir] day of death, and their heart is as sound as a palace.¹⁶ ¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁶ They are always thinking about joy and levity which brings a person to sin. (Metsudas Dovid)
¹⁷ Alternatively, the heart of fools is involved in mockery, in eating, drinking and seeking enjoyment, and they have no concern over the suffering of their brethren. (Targum)



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:6:1)


**כִּי כְקוֹל הַסִּירִים.** עֲצֵי קוֹצִים, אשפינ"ט בְּלַעַ"ז:


**For like the crackling of thorns.** Thorn wood, *espines* in O.F.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:6:2)


**תַּחַת הַסִּיר.** תַּחַת סִיר נְחשֶׁת הַכָּפוּי עַל תַּבְעֵרַת קוֹצִים, וְהֵם מְקַשְׁקְשִׁים לְתוֹכָהּ. אָמַר רַבִּי יְהוֹשֻׁעַ בֶּן לֵוִי: כָּל קִיסַיָּא, כַּד אִינוּן דָּלְקִין, לֵית קָלְהוֹן אָזִיל. בְּרַם, הָלֵין סִירַיָּתָא, קָלְהוֹן אָזִיל, לְמֵימָר, אַף אֲנָן מִן קִיסַיָּא. מוֹדִיעִים שֶׁאַף אָנוּ מִן הָעֵצִים, וְיֵשׁ צוֹרֶךְ בָּנוּ. אַף הַכְּסִילִים מַרְבִּים דְּבָרִים, לוֹמַר: "גַּם אָנוּ מִן הַחֲשׁוּבִים":


**Under a pot.** Under a copper pot turned over a fire of thorns, and they rattle into it.¹⁸ Rabbi Yehoshua the son of Leivi said, that all [other] woods when they are kindled, their sound does not travel [far], but when thorns are kindled, their sound travels [far], as if to say, “We, too, are of wood”. They publicize, “We too are wood, and we are needed.” So do fools speak excessively, [as if] to say, “We, too, are important.”

footnotes:
¹⁸ The crackling of thorns in the fire lasts only a brief moment and then is gone. So, too, the laughter of the fool has no useful purpose or lasting value. Or, the verse conveys the thought that the wise grieve because they are fearful that they are lacking in their service to God, whereas fools are generally satisfied and happy because they feel they have totally fulfilled their obligation in their service to God.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:6:3)


**וְגַּם זֶה הָבֶל.** וְעָמָל הוּא, שֶׁמָּסַר הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא לַבְּרִיּוֹת לִהְיוֹת יְגֵעִים וּמְקַנְתְּרִים בָּהֶם:


**This too is vanity.** And it is labor, which the Holy One, Blessed Is He, gave the people to toil in and to be vexed with.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:7:1)


**כִּי הָעשֶׁק יְהוֹלֵל חָכָם.** כְּשֶׁהַכְּסִיל מְקַנְתֵּר אֶת הֶחָכָם, מְעַרְבֵּב דַּעְתּוֹ, וְגַם הוּא נִכְשָׁל. דָּתָן וַאֲבִירָם קִנְתְּרוּ אֶת משֶׁה, לוֹמַר: "יֵרֶא ה' עֲלֵיכֶם וְיִשְׁפֹּט וְגוֹ'", וִיְעִרְבְּבוּהוּ וְאִבְּדוּ אֶת לִבּוֹ, וְגָרְמוּ לוֹ שֶׁהִקְפִּיד כְּנֶגֶד הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא, וְאָמַר, "וְהַצֵּל לֹא הִצַּלְתָּ אֶת עַמֶּךָ", וְנֶעֱנַשׁ בַּדָּבָר, שֶׁהֱשִׁיבוֹ, "עַתָּה תִרְאֶה", וְלֹא תִרְאֶה בְמִלְחֶמֶת ל"א מְלָכִים:


**For quarreling makes the wise foolish.** When the fool provokes the wise person, he confuses his thoughts, and he too, stumbles. Dasan and Aviram taunted Moshe, by saying, “may Adonoy look down upon you and judge, etc.,”¹⁹ and they confused him and caused his wisdom to depart and to speak in anger²⁰ against the Holy One, Blessed Is He, and he said, “and You did not save Your people,”²¹ and he was punished for this matter, when He replied to him, “now you will see,”²² but you will not see the war of the thirty-one kings.

footnotes:
¹⁹ Shemos 5:21.
²⁰ A wise person should distance himself from being involved in discussion with fools because it will cause his own wisdom to suffer. (Metsudas Dovid)
²¹ Ibid. Verse 23.
²² Ibid. 6:1.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:7:2)


**לֵב מַתָּנָה.** לֵב חָכְמָה, שֶׁהִיא מַתָּנָה לְאָדָם, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר, "כִּי ה' יִתֵּן חָכְמָה":


**And a gift [corrupts] the heart.** The heart of wisdom, which is a gift to man,²³ as it is stated, “For Adonoy gives wisdom.”²⁴

footnotes:
²³ I.e., the provocation by the wicked, causes the wise to forfeit their wisdom, which is God‘s gift to man.
²⁴ Mishlei 2:6.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:7:3)


**עשֶׁק.** לְשׁוֹן רִיב וְקִנְתּוּרִין. יֵשׁ פָּנִים אֲחֵרִים מַפְרִידִין הַמִּקְרָאוֹת זוּ מִזּוּ וְ"כִי" הָאָמוּר בְּרֹאשׁ הַמִּקְרָא מוֹכִיחַ שֶׁמְּחֻבָּר לַמִּקְרָא שֶׁלְּפָנָיו:


**Quarreling.** An expression of conflict and provocation. There are other interpretations, but they separate the verses one from the other, but “כִּי” [=for] which is stated at the beginning of the verse proves that it is connected to the preceding verse.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:8:1)


**טוֹב אַחֲרִית דָּבָר מֵרֵאשִׁיתוֹ.** כְּמַשְׁמָעוֹ, בְּרֵאשִׁית הַדָּבָר, אֵין אָנוּ יוֹדְעִים מַה יְּהֵא בְסוֹפוֹ, אֲבָל כְּשֶׁאַחֲרִית טוֹב, נִגְמַר בְּטוֹב.


**Better is the end of a matter than its beginning.** [To be interpreted] according to its apparent meaning, [i.e.,] at the beginning of a matter, we do not know what will be its outcome,²⁵ but when the outcome is good, it ends well.

footnotes:
²⁵ And we are therefore unable to evaluate it properly.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:8:2)


דָּבָר אַחֵר: **טוֹב אַחֲרִית דָּבָר מֵרֵאשִׁיתוֹ.** בִּזְמַן שֶׁהוּא מֵרֵאשִׁיתוֹ טוֹב, שֶׁנִּתְכַּוְּנוּ לְטוֹבָה כְּשֶׁהִתְתִילוּ בּוֹ. רַבִּי מֵאִיר הֲוָה קָאִים וְדָרִישׁ כּוּלֵיהּ מִלְתָא בֶּאֱלִישָׁע בֶּן אֲבוּיָה בְּמִדְרַשׁ קֹהֶלֶת:


Another explanation: **Better is the end of a matter than its beginning.** When it is good from its beginning, [i.e.,] that they had good intentions when they started it.²⁶ Rabbi Meir stood and expounded the entire matter as referring to Elisha the son of Avuyah,²⁷) in Midrash Koheles.

footnotes:
²⁶ Alternatively, immediately at the beginning of a matter, a person should reflect on its repercussions, and by doing so he will constantly improve himself, as the Gemara in Maseches Tamid 32a states, “Who is wise? He who discerns what is about to come to pass.” (Ksav Sofer)
²⁷ Rashi should not be translated literally, that Rabbi Meir expounded this matter etc., rather Rabbi Meir was in the midst of expounding when Elisha clarified to Rabbi Meir what Rabbi Akiva expounded pertaining to this verse. (Sifsei Chachomim


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:8:3)


**אֶרֶךְ רוּחַ.** הַמַּאֲרִיךְ רָגְזוֹ, וְאֵינוֹ מְמַהֵר לָרִיב:


**Patience.** One who is slow to anger and is not easily provoked.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁸ See Mishnayos Avos 5:11.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:9:1)


**אַל תְּבַהֵל.** אַל תְּמַהֵר:


**Do not quicken.** Do not rush.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:10:1)


**אַל תֹּאמַר מֶה הָיָה שֶׁהַיָּמִים וְגוֹ'.** אַל תִּתְמַהּ עַל הַטּוֹבָה שֶׁהָיְתָה בָאָה עַל הַצַּדִּיקִים הָרִאשׁוֹנִים, כְּדוֹר הַמִּדְבָּר וְדוֹרוֹ שֶׁל יְהוֹשֻׁעַ וְדוֹרוֹ שֶׁל דָּוִד:


**Do not say, was it that the former days, etc.** Do not wonder about the goodness that was bestowed upon the righteous men of yore, such as the generation of the wilderness, the generation of Yehoshua and the generation of Dovid.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:10:2)


**כִּי לֹא מֵחָכְמָה שָׁאַלְתָּ.** שֶׁהַכֹּל לְפִי זְכוּת הַדּוֹרוֹת:


**For it is not out of wisdom that you ask.** For everything depends upon the merit of the generations.²⁹

footnotes:
²⁹ Alternatively, the answer to this question is beyond human comprehension. (Metsudas Dovid)



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:11:1)


**טוֹבָה חָכְמָה וְגוֹ'.** חָכְמָתָם עָמְדָה לָהֶם עִם נַחֲלַת זְכוּת אֲבוֹתָם, כִּי טוֹבָה הִיא הַחָכְמָה:


**It is good to have wisdom, etc.** Their wisdom stood for them together with the heritage of the merit of their forefathers, for wisdom is good.³⁰

footnotes:
³⁰ Alternatively, נחלה refers to a monetary inheritance or a skill that is handed down from father to son (Ibn Ezra). Thereby, one is able to immerse himself in his studies without having the burden of financial worries (Alshich). See Mishnayos Avos 2:2 which states that the study of Torah is good when combined with an occupation, for the exertion of them both makes sin forgotten. Or, wisdom is good as an accompaniment to an inheritance, for without wisdom, the wealth he came into will not endure. (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:11:2)


**וְיֹתֵר לְרֹאֵי הַשָּׁמֶשׁ.** יִתְרוֹן הִיא הַחָכְמָה לְכָל הַבְּרִיּוֹת:


**And [such wisdom] benefits those who see the sun.** Wisdom is a benefit for all mankind.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:11:3)


**לְרֹאֵי הַשָּׁמֶשׁ.** הֵן כָּל הַבְּרִיּוֹת, כְּדִתְנַן: "הַנּוֹדֵר מֵרוֹאֵי הַחַמָּה אָסוּר אַף בְּסוּמִין. לֹא נִתְכַּוֵּן זֶה אֶלָּא בְמִי שֶׁהַחַמָּה רוֹאָה אוֹתוֹ:


**Those who see the sun.** This refers to all mankind, as it was taught in the Mishnah,³¹ “He who vows not to benefit from those who see the sun, is also forbidden [to benefit] from the blind.” The intention [of this phrase] is to [include] anyone who benefits from the sun.

footnotes:
³¹ Mishnayos Nedarim 3:7.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:12:1)


**כִּי בְּצֵל הַחָכְמָה בְּצֵל הַכָּסֶף.** כָּל מִי שֶׁיֶּשְׁנוֹ בְצֵל הַחָכְמָה יֶשְׁנוֹ בְצֵל הַכָּסֶף, שֶׁהַחָכְמָה גוֹרֶמֶת לְעשֶׁר שֶׁיָּבֹא:


**For wisdom shelters [as well] as money shelters.** Whoever is in the shade of wisdom is in the shade of money, for wisdom causes eventual wealth.³²

footnotes:
³² Alternatively, just as wisdom of the Torah gives life [i.e., shelters] in the World of Come, so does money that is given to charity provide life [i.e., shelters] in the World to Come. (Targum)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:12:2)


**וְיִתְרוֹן דַּעַת הַחָכְמָה תְּחַיֶּה בְעָלֶיהָ.** וְעוֹד הַחָכְמָה יְתֵרָה עַל הַכֶּסֶף, שֶׁהַחָכְמָה תְּחַיֶּה בְעָלֶיהָ:


**But the advantage of knowledge is that wisdom preserves the life of him who possesses it.** And moreover, wisdom has an advantage over money, because wisdom preserves the life of its possessors.³³

footnotes:
³³ Wisdom is not subject to death or consumption. The idea that “wisdom preserves the life of him who possesses it,” may be in reference to the Gemara in Maseches Yevamos 97a, which states that the lips of a deceased scholar in whose name a traditional statement is reported in this world, move gently in the grave. (Reb Tzvi Charif)



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:13:1)


**רְאֵה אֶת מַעֲשֵׂה הָאֱלֹהִים.** הֵיאַךְ הוּא מְתֻקָּן, הַכֹּל לְפִי הַפְּעֻלָּה שֶׁל אָדָם: גַּן עֵדֶן לַצַּדִּיקִים, גֵיהִנֹּם לָרְשָׁעִים. רְאֵה לְךָ בְּאֵיזֶה תִדְבָּק:


**Consider the deeds of God.** How everything is prepared according to man’s actions, the Garden of Eden for the righteous and Gehinnom for the wicked. Consider to which you should cling.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:13:2)


**כִּי מִי יוּכַל לְתַקֵּן.** לְאַחַר מִיתָה אֶת הַדָּבָר אֲשֶׁר עִוְּתוֹ בְחַיָּיו:


**For who [else] can straighten.** After death that which he made crooked during his life?³⁴

footnotes:
³⁴ Alternatively, only God can straighten what He made crooked on account of the inequity of the generation, e.g., the Gemara in Maseches Rosh Hashanah 17b states, that if little rain had been decreed on account of the inequities of the generation and afterwards the people improved their ways, God will rectify what He made crooked and cause the rain to fall in its proper time and place without the loss of a drop. (Metsudas Dovid)



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:14:1)


**בְּיוֹם טוֹבָה הֱיֵה בְטוֹב.** בְּיוֹם שֶׁיֵּשׁ בְּיָדְךָ לַעֲשׂוֹת טוֹבָה, הֱיֵה בְעוֹשֵׂי הַטּוֹבָה:


**On a day of goodness [prosperity], be in good spirits.** On a day in which you have the ability to do good,³⁵ be among those who do good.

footnotes:
³⁵ Alternatively, on a day of good fortune be happy and enjoy it. (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:14:2)


**וּבְיוֹם רָעָה רְאֵה.** כְּשֶׁתָּבוֹא הָרָעָה עַל הָרְשָׁעִים, אַתָּה תִהְיֶה מִן הָרוֹאִים, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר, "וְיָצְאוּ וְרָאוּ בְּפִגְרֵי הָאֲנָשִׁים וְגוֹ'", וְלֹא תִהְיֶה מִן הַנִּרְאִים: "וְהָיוּ דֵרָאוֹן לְכָל בָּשָׂר":


**And on a day of adversity reflect.** When evil comes upon the wicked, be among the observers,³⁶ as it is stated, “And they will go out and see the corpses of the people, etc.,”³⁷ and you will not be among those who are seen, “and they will be a sight for all mankind.”³⁸

footnotes:
³⁶ Adversity is caused by being sinful. Therefore, examine your deeds and remove any sins by repenting (Sforno). Alternatively, on a day of adversity, reflect and be hopeful for a day of goodness to come. (Likutei Yehudah)
³⁷ Yeshayahu 66:24.
³⁸ Ibid.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:14:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:14:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:14:3)


**גַּם אֶת זֶה לְעֻמַּת זֶה.** הַטּוֹבָה וּשְׂכַר פְּעֻלָּתָהּ לְעֻמַּת הָרָעָה וּשְׂכַר פְּעֻלָּתָהּ:


**The one was set up against the other.** The good and the reward for its performance, in contrast to the evil and the ensuing punishment for doing it.³⁹

footnotes:
³⁹ Alternatively, “one set up against the other,” refers to the punishment that was set up against the evil deeds, i.e., the concept of מדה כנגד מדה, that is, the manner of punishment corresponds to the sin, and it is never meted out in a greater amount than warranted.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:14:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:14:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:14:4)


**שֶׁלֹּא יִמְצָא הָאָדָם אַחֲרָיו מְאוּמָה.** לְהַרְהֵר אַחֲרָיו שֶׁל הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא:


**That man should not find anything after him.** To be critical of the Holy One, Blessed Is He.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:15:1)


**יֵשׁ צַדִּיק אֹבֵד בְּצִדְקוֹ.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהוּא אוֹבֵד, עוֹדֶנּוּ עוֹמֵד בְּצִדְקוֹ. מַעֲשֵׂה בְּיוֹסֵף בֶּן פִּנְחָס הַכֹּהֵן שֶׁעָלְתָה נִימָא בְרַגְלוֹ. קָרְאוּ לְרוֹפֵא לַחֲתּוֹךְ רַגְלוֹ. אָמַר לֵיהּ: "כְּשֶׁאַתָּה מַגִּיעַ לְחוּט הַשַּׂעֲרָה, הוֹדִיעֵנִי". וְכֵן עָשָׂה, קָרָא לְחוּנְיָא בְּנוֹ, אָמַר לוֹ: "בְּנִי, עַד כַּאן הָיִיתָ חַיָּב לִטַּפֵּל בִּי. מִכַּאן וְאֵילֵךְ אֵין אַתָּה חַיָּב לִטַּפֵּל בִּי, שֶׁאֵין כֹּהֵן מִטַּמֵּא לְאֵבֶר מִן הַחַי מֵאָבִיו:


**There is a righteous man who perishes [despite] his righteousness.** Although he is perishing, he is nonetheless steadfast in his righteousness.⁴⁰ There was an incident concerning Yoseif the son of Pinchas the *kohen* who had an ulcerating sore on his leg. They called the physician to amputate his leg. He said to him, “when you are about to reach the last remaining hairbreadth [of completing the amputation], let me know,” and he did so. He then called his son Chunya and said to him, “My son , until now, you were obligated to care for me. But, from now on, you are no longer obligated to care for me, because a *kohen* may not become ritually impure from a limb cut off from his father while he was alive.”

footnotes:
⁴⁰ Alternatively, “there is a righteous man who perishes because of his righteousness,” i.e., were he not righteous, he might not have been punished at all. God is more demanding with the righteous, as stated in Maseches Yevamos 121b (Metsudas Dovid and Alshich). Avrohom, Yaakov, Yoseif, Dovid, Michal and Chizkiyah are examples of righteous people who did not escape judgement because of a slight indiscretion on their part. (Mesilas Yeshorim)



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:16:1)


**אַל תְּהִי צַדִּיק הַרְבֵּה.** כְּשָׁאוּל, שֶׁדִּמָּה לִהְיוֹת צַדִּיק וְרִחֵם עַל הָרְשָׁעִים:


**Be not exceedingly righteous.** Like Shaul, who tried to display his righteousness and [misdirected] his mercy on the wicked.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:16:2)


**וְאַל תִּתְחַכַּם יוֹתֵר.** לָדוּן קַל וָחֹמֶר שֶׁל שְׁטוּת, וּמַה עַל נֶפֶשׁ אַחַת אָמְרָה תוֹרָה, הָבֵא עֶגְלָה עֲרוּפָה, וְכוּ':


**Nor overly wise.** To deduce from a foolish inference from a minor case to a major case, [namely,] that for [the murder] of one person, said the Torah, bring a decapitated heifer, etc.⁴¹

footnotes:
⁴¹ This Rashi refers back to the preceding one and explains the misdirected logic which led Shaul to spare Amoleik.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:17:1)


**אַל תִּרְשַׁע הַרְבֵּה.** אֲפִלּוּ רָשַׁעְתָּ מְעַט, אַל תּוֹסִיף לְהַרְשִׁיעַ:


**Be not excessively wicked.** Even if you have been somewhat wicked, do not continue to act wickedly.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:17:2)


**לָמָּה תָמוּת בְּלֹא עִתֶּךָ.** כְּשָׁאוּל, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: "וַיָּמָת שָׁאוּל בְּמַעֲלוֹ אֲשֶׁר מָעַל" בְּנֹב עִיר הַכֹּהֲנִים וּבַעֲמָלֵק:


**Why should you die before your time.** As Shaul did, as it is stated, “and Shaul died for his transgressions that he committed [against Adonoy],”⁴² in the case of Nov, the city of *kohanim*,⁴³ and in the case of Amoleik.⁴⁴

footnotes:
⁴² I Divrei Hayomim 10:13.
⁴³ See I Shmuel 22:16-19.
⁴⁴ See I Shmuel 15:8-15.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:18:1)


**טוֹב אֲשֶׁר תֶּאֱחֹז בָּזֶה וְגוֹ'.** אֱחוֹז בְּצֶדֶק וּבְרֶשַׁע. אִם אָמַר הַנָּבִיא הַצַּדִיק דָּבָר שֶׁהוּא דוֹמֶה לְךָ לְרֶשַׁע, כְּגוֹן שֶׁאָמַר שְׁמוּאֵל לְשָׁאוּל, אַל יֵקַל בְּעֵינֶיךָ לְפַקְפֵק בּוֹ:


**It is best to take hold of one, etc.** Grasp both, righteousness and wickedness. If the righteous prophet told you something that appears to you as being evil, e.g., what Shmuel said to Shaul, let it not be taken lightly by you to doubt it.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:18:2)


**יֵצֵא אֶת כֻּלָּם.** יְדֵי שְׁנֵיהֶם, יְקַיֵּם הַצֶּדֶק וְהָרֶשַׁע כְּהִלְכָתָן:


**Will discharge his duty to both.** Both of them, to preserve the righteousness and the wickedness according to their rule.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:19:1)


**הַחָכְמָה תָּעֹז לֶחָכָם.** לְפִי שֶׁאָמַר, "אַל תִּרְשַׁע הַרְבֵּה", אִם רָשַׁעְתָּ מְעַט, אַל תּוֹסִיף עָלָיו, אֶלָּא פַּשְׁפֵּשׁ בְּמַעֲשֶׂיךָ וּתְהֵא תוֹהֵא עַל הַחֵטְא, אָמַר, "הַחָכְמָה תָּעֹז לֶחָכָם", שֶׁהִיא יוֹעַצְתּוֹ לָשׁוּב בִּתְשׁוּבָה:


**Wisdom gives [more] strength to the wise.** Since it stated, “be not excessively wicked,”⁴⁵ if you have been somewhat wicked, do not add to it, rather examine your deeds and regret the sin. He states, “wisdom gives [more] strength to the wise,” because it leads him to repentance.⁴⁶)

footnotes:
⁴⁵ Above, verse 17.
⁴⁶ Rashi explains the juxtaposition of verse 17 with this verse is to teach that in the event a person sinned slightly, his wisdom will encourage him to repent his way. (Sifsei Chachomim


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:19:2)


**מֵעֲשָׂרָה שַׁלִּיטִים.** מָצִינוּ בְיֹאשִׁיָּהוּ שֶׁהֵעִיד עָלָיו הַכָּתוּב, "וְכָמֹהוּ לֹא הָיָה לְפָנָיו מֶלֶךְ וְגוֹ'". הֲרֵי עָמְדָה לוֹ חָכְמָתוֹ, שֶׁפִּשְׁפֵּשׁ בְמַעֲשָׂיו וְטוֹב לוֹ מֵעֲשָׂרָה מְלָכִים שֶׁהִרְשִׁיעוּ וְלֹא שָׁבוּ מִדַּרְכָּם:


**Than ten rulers.** We find regarding Yoshiyahu, that Scripture testified about him, “the likes of him there had never been a king before him, etc.”⁴⁷ Therefore, his wisdom remained with him, and he examined his actions, and it was better for him than “ten rulers” who acted wickedly and did not repent.⁴⁸

footnotes:
⁴⁷ II Melochim 23:25.
⁴⁸ Alternatively, the חכם refers to Yoseif, and the ten rulers refer to Yoseif’s brothers. (Targum)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:19:3)


**אֲשֶׁר הָיוּ בָּעִיר.** בִּיְרוּשָׁלַיִם: רְחַבְעָם, אֲבִיָּה, אֲחַזְיָה, יוֹאָשׁ אַחֲרֵי מוֹת יְהוֹיָדָע, אֲמַצְיָה, אָחָז, מְנַשֶּׁה, אָמוֹן, יְהוֹיָקִים, צִדְקִיָּהוּ:


**Of the city.** In Yerusholayim. [The ten were,] Rechovam, Aviyah, Achazyah, Yo’ash, after the death of Yehoyada,⁴⁹) Amatzyah, Achaz, Menashe, Amon, Yehoyakim, and Tzidkiyahu.

footnotes:
⁴⁹ For as long as Yehoyada was alive, Yo’ash was completely righteous. (Sifsei Chachomim



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:20:1)


**כִּי אָדָם אֵין צַדִּיק בָּאָרֶץ.** לְפִיכָךְ, צָרִיךְ לְפַשְׁפֵּשׁ בְּמַעֲשָׂיו:


**For there is not a righteous man on earth.** Therefore, he must examine his actions.⁵⁰

footnotes:
⁵⁰ Rashi is explaining the previous verse [Above 7:19], where one could question that instead of stating that wisdom encourages a person to repent, would not wisdom deter one from sinning altogether. Therefore Scripture declares that “there is not a righteous man...who...never sins.”



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:1)


**גַּם לְכָל הַדְּבָרִים וְגוֹ'.** לְפִי שֶׁמְּדַבֵּר בְּשָׁאוּל שֶׁקִּבֵּל לְשׁוֹן הָרַע עַל נוֹב עִיר הַכֹּהֲנִים, וְעָלָיו נֶאֱמַר: "אַל תִּרְשַׁע הַרְבֵּה", אָמַר: "גַּם לְכָל הַדְבָרִים וְגוֹ'", אֲשֶׁר יְדַּבְּרוּ אֵלֶיךָ הוֹלְכֵי רָכִיל, אַל תִּתֵּן לִבְּךָ לְקַבְּלָן:


**Furthermore, of all the words, etc.** Because he is speaking of Shaul, who accepted slander regarding Nov the city of *kohanim*, and concerning him it is stated, “ Be not excessively wicked,”⁵¹ he says, “Furthermore of all the words that are spoken,” to you by talebearers, “take them not to heart,” to accept them.

footnotes:
⁵¹ Above, verse 17.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:2)


**אֲשֶׁר לֹא תִשְׁמַע אֶת עַבְדְּךָ מְקַלְּלֶךָ.** אֵין טוֹב אֲשֶׁר תַּטֶּה אָזְנְךָ לִשְׁמוֹעַ אֶת עַבְדְּךָ מְקַלְּלֶךָ.


**Lest you hear your own servant curse you.** It is not good that you incline your ear to hear your servant cursing you.⁵²)

footnotes:
⁵² As Dovid did with Shimi the son of Geira. See II Shmuel 16:5-13. (Sifsei Chachomim


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:3)


דָּבָר אַחֵר: **מֵעֲשָׂרָה שַׁלִּיטִים.** אֵלּוּ עֲשָׂרָה דְבָרִים הַמְחַיְּבִים אֶת הָאָדָם: שְׁתֵּי עֵינָיו מָרְאִין אוֹתוֹ דִבְרֵי עֲבֵרָה. שְׁתֵּי אָזְנָיו מַשְׁמִיעִין אוֹתוֹ דְבָרִים בְּטֵלִים. שְׁתֵּי יָדָיו שֶׁגּוֹזֵל וְחוֹמֵס בָּהֶן. שְׁתֵּי רַגְלָיו שֶׁמוֹלִיכִין אוֹתוֹ לִדְבַר עֲבֵרָה. וּפִיו וְלִבּוֹ.


Another explanation: **Than ten rulers.** These are the ten things that condemn a person. His two eyes show him sinful things, his two ears which enable him to hear idle talk, his two hands with which he robs and plunders, his two feet which lead him to sinful things, and his mouth and his heart.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:21:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:4)


דָּבָר אַחֵר: **הַחָכְמָה תָּעֹז לֶחָכָם.** זֶה נֹחַ. **מֵּעֲשָׂרָה שַׁלִּיטִים.** מֵעֲשָׂרָה דוֹרוֹת שֶׁלְּפָנָיו.


Another explanation: **Wisdom gives [more] strength to the wise.** This is No’ach. **Than ten rulers.** More than the ten generations that were before him.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:21:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:5)


דָּבָר אַחֵר: **לֶחָכָם.** זֶה אָבְרָהָם. **מֵעֲשָׂרָה שַׁלִּיטִים.** מֵעֲשָׂרָה דוֹרוֹת שֶׁלְפָנָיו.


Another explanation: **To the wise.** This is Avrohom. **Than ten rulers.** More than the ten generations that were before him.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:21:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:6)


דָּבָר אַחֵר: **לֶחָכָם.** זֶה יוֹסֵף. **מֵעֲשָׂרָה שַׁלִּיטִים.** אֵלּוּ אֶחָיו.


Another explanation: **To the wise.** This is Yoseif. **Than ten rulers.** These are his brothers.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:21:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:21:7)


דָּבָר אַחֵר: **לֶחָכָם.** זֶה משֶׁה. **מֵעֲשָׂרָה שַׁלִּיטִים.** עֲשָׂרָה דְבָרִים הַמְּשַׁמְשִׁין אֶת הַגּוּף עַל יְדֵי מַאֲכָל: מִן פּוּמָא לְוֶשְׁטָא וּמִן וֶשְׁטָא לְכַרְסָא וְכוּ' כִּדְאִיתָא בְמִדְרַשׁ קֹהֶלֶת. וְחָכְמָתוֹ עָמְדָה לוֹ לְמשֶׁה שֶׁלֹּא נִצְטָרֵךְ לְמַאֲכָל אַרְבָּעִים יוֹם וְאַרְבָּעִים לָיְלָה. כָּל הַפָּנִים הַלָּלוּ בַמִּדְרָשׁ, וְאֵינִי יָכוֹל לְיַשֵּׁב עֲלֵיהֶן מִקְרָא שֶׁלְּאַחֲרָיו: "כִּי אָדָם אֵין צַּדִּיק בָּאָרֶץ".


Another explanation: **To the wise.** This is Moshe. **Than ten rulers.** [The] ten organs that minister to food ingested by the body. From his mouth to the esophagus, from the esophagus to the stomach, etc., as it is [stated] in Midrash Koheles, but Moshe’s wisdom stood for him so that he did not need food for forty days and forty nights. All these interpretations are in the Midrash, but I am unable to reconcile them with the verse following, “For there is not a righteous man on earth.”⁵³

footnotes:
⁵³ Above, verse 20.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:23:1)


**כָּל זֹה נִסִּיתִי בַחָכְמָה** מוּסָב עַל מִקְרָאוֹת שֶׁלְּמַטָּן (סְפָרִים אֲחֵרִים: וְלֹא) וְעַל שֶׁלְּמַעְלָן:


“All this I have probed with wisdom,”⁵⁴ refers to the verses below (other texts: and not) and [to the verses] above.

footnotes:
⁵⁴ Below, verse 23.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:23:2)


**נִסִּיתִי בַחָכְמָה.** בַּתּוֹרָה:


**I have probed with wisdom.** With the [wisdom of the] Torah.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:23:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:23:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:23:3)


**אָמַרְתִּי אֶחְכָּמָה.** לָדַעַת אֶת הַחָכְמָה:


**I said I will be wise.** To know the wisdom [of Torah].⁵⁵

footnotes:
⁵⁵ Alternatively, if I consider myself a wise person [=אמרתי אחכמה], then it is beyond doubt that wisdom is very distant from me [=והיא רחוקה ממני]. (Rabbi of Kotzk)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:23:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:23:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:23:4)


**וְהִיא רְחוֹקָה מִמֶּנִּי.** וּמָה הִיא זוּ?:


**But [it proved to be] far beyond me.** And what is this?⁵⁶

footnotes:
⁵⁶ Alternatively, the reason why the Torah proved to be far, i.e., I am unable to comprehend it, is because of me [=ממני] and not, God forbid, that the Torah is lacking. (Amtachas Binyomin)



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:24:1)


**רָחוֹק מַה שֶׁהָיָה.** אֶת הַדְּבָרִים הָרְחוֹקִים שֶׁהָיוּ בִיצִירַת בְּרֵאשִׁית:


**That which was is far beyond me.** The distant things that were in the Creation.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:24:2)


**וְעָמֹק עָמֹק הוּא "מִי יִמְצָאֶנּוּ"**, שֶׁאֵין לִי רְשׁוּת לְהַרְהֵר בָּהֶן, מַה לְּמַעְלָה וּמַה לְּמַטָּה, מַה לְּפָנִים וּמַה לְּאָחוֹר: וְעוֹד:


**And so very deep it is; who can find it,** for I am not permitted to delve into them, what is above and what is below, what is before and what is after.⁵⁷ Furthermore—

footnotes:
⁵⁷ See Maseches Chagigah 11b.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:25:1)


**סַּבּוֹתִי.** לָדַעַת וְלָתוּר וּלְבַקֵּשׁ חָכְמַת פָּרָשַׁת פָּרָה אֲדֻמָּה וְחֶשְׁבּוֹן קֵץ הַגְּאֻלָּה:


**So I turned about.** To know and to search out and to seek the wisdom [=meaning] of the section dealing with the Red Heifer⁵⁸ ⁵⁹ and the computation of the End [of exile] for Redemption.

footnotes:
⁵⁸ The numerical value [gematria] of “והיא רחוקה” equals 341, the same as “פרה אדומה.”
⁵⁹ The Gemara in Maseches Yoma 14a states, that verse 7:23 above “I said, ‘I will be wise but it proved far beyond [והיא רחוקה] me,’” is referring to the Red Heifer, whose ashes render ritually clean one who has become contaminated by contact with a corpse, and yet these same ashes contaminate one who is ritually clean.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:25:2)


**וְלָדַעַת רֶשַׁע כֶּסֶל.** לַעֲמֹד עַל סוֹף דַּעְתָּה שֶׁל אַפִּיקוֹרְסוּת וְסִכְלוּת הוֹלֵלוֹת, מְעֻרְבָּב וּמְשֻּׁעְמָם שֶׁבָּהּ:


**And to know that wickedness is folly.** To foresee the ultimate end of heresy, and “foolishness is madness,” [i.e.,] the confusion and madness therein.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:26:1)


**וּמוֹצֶא אֲנִי מַר מִמָּוֶת.** שֶׁהִיא קָשָׁה מֵעֲשָׂרָה דְבָרִים הַקָּשִׁים שֶׁנִּבְרְאוּ בָעוֹלָם, כִּדְאִיתָא בְּבָבָא בַּתְרָא בְּ"הַשֻּׁתָּפִין". "וּמוֹצֶא אֲנִי מַר" וְקָשֶׁה מִמֶּנָּה "אֶת הָאִשָּׁה", זוּ הַמִּנוּת:


**And I find more bitter than death.** Which is the severest of the ten harsh things that were created in the world, as it is [stated] in [Maseches] Bava Basra, in [the chapter entitled] “The Partners.”⁶⁰ “And I find more bitter” and severe than it, “the woman,” this refers to heresy.

footnotes:
⁶⁰ 10a.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:26:2)


**וַחֲרָמִים לִבָּהּ.** לְשׁוֹן מִכְמֹרֶת, כְּמוֹ, "יְגֹרֵהוּ בְחֶרְמוֹ וְיַאַסְפֵהוּ בְּמִכְמַרְתּוֹ":


**Whose her heart is full of [traps and] nets.** An expression of a net, as in, “he catches them in his net and gathers them in his trawl.”⁶¹

footnotes:
⁶¹ Chavakuk 1:15.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:26:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:26:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:26:3)


**אֲסוּרִים יָדֶיהָ.** וּמִשֶּׁהֶחֱזִיקָה בְאָדָם, הֲרֵי הוּא כְנִקְשַׁר בְּקִשּׁוּרֵי עֲבוֹתוֹת:


**Her hands as chains.** Once she seizes a man, he is as though bound with thick ropes.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:26:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:26:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:26:4)


**אֲסוּרִים.** שֵׁם דָּבָר שֶׁל קִּשׁוּרִים, כְּמוֹ "וַיִּמַּסוּ אֱסוּרָיו", קִשּׁוּרָיו. וְכֵן פֵּרְשׁוֹ מְנַחֵם:


**Chains.** אֲסוּרִים is] a noun meaning “bonds,” as in, “and his bonds אֱסוּרָיו melted,”⁶² meaning his bonds, and so did Menachem explain it.

footnotes:
⁶² Shoftim 15:14.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:27:1)


**רְאֵה זֶה מָצָאתִי אָמְרָה קֹהֶלֶת אַחַת לְאַחַת לִמְצֹא חֶשְׁבּוֹן.** כָּל הַמִּצְוֹת שֶׁהַצַּדִּיקִים עוֹשִׂים וְהָעֲבֵרוֹת שֶׁהָרְשָׁעִים עוֹבְרִים נִמְנִים לִפְנֵי הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא אַחַת אֶל אַחַת עַד שֶׁמִּצְטָרְפוֹת לְחֶשְׁבּוֹן גָּדוֹל. כַּךְ פֵּרְשׁוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ בְמַסֶּכֶת סוֹטָה:


**See, this is what I have found, says Koheles, adding one to another to find an accounting.** All the commandments that the righteous perform, and the transgressions that the wicked commit, are counted before the Holy One, Blessed Is He, one [is placed next] to the other, and they combine to a large amount. So did our Rabbis explain it in Maseches Sotah.⁶³

footnotes:
⁶³ 8b.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:27:2)


**אָמְרָה קֹהֶלֶת.** אָמְרָה קְבוּצַת הַחָכְמָה, וְאָמְרָה נַפְשׁוֹ הַמַּשְׂכֶּלֶת הַמְקַבֶּצֶת הַחָכְמָה:


**Says Koheles.** Said the collection of wisdom, [i.e.,] said his intellectual soul which collects [his] wisdom.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:27:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:27:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:27:3)


**קֹהֶלֶת.** לְשׁוֹן נְקֵבָה הִיא, וּכְשֶׁהוּא אוֹמְרוֹ בִלְשׁוֹן זָכָר, מוּסָב עַל הַקּוֹבְצָהּ, וְהוּא שְׁלֹמֹה. וּבַמִּדְרָשׁ מָצִינוּ: אָמַר רַבִּי יִרְמְיָה בֶּן אֶלְעָזָר: רוּחַ הַקֹּדֶשׁ פְּעָמִים מְשִׂיחָה בִלְשׁוֹן זָכָר, וּפְעָמִים מְשִׂיחָה בִלְשׁוֹן נְקֵבָה. כָּתוּב אֶחָד אוֹמֵר "עֶזְרִי וּמְפַלְטִי אַתָּה", וְכָתוּב אֶחָד אוֹמֵר "עֶזְרָתִי וּמְפַלְטִי אַתָּה". כָּתוּב אֶחָד אוֹמֵר "מְבַשֵּׂר מַשְׁמִיעַ שָׁלוֹם", וְכָתוּב אֶחָד אוֹמֵר "קוֹל מְבַשֶּׂרֶת צִיּוֹן":


**Koheles.** This קֹהֶלֶת is a feminine noun, and when it is used in the masculine form, it refers to the one who gathers it, i.e., Shlomo. We find in the Midrash, Rabbi Yirmiyah the son of Elazar said, that the Holy Spirit sometimes speaks in the masculine gender and sometimes it speaks in the feminine gender. One verse states, “you are my help עֶזְרִי and my rescuer,”⁶⁴ and another verse states, “you are my help עֶזְרָתִי and my rescuer.”⁶⁵ [Also,] one verse states, “a herald מְבַשֵּׂר proclaiming peace,”⁶⁶ and another verse states, (the voice of)⁶⁷ “the herald מְבַשֶּׂרֶת of Tzion.”⁶⁸

footnotes:
⁶⁴ Tehillim 70:6.
⁶⁵ Ibid. 40:18.
⁶⁶ Nachum 2:1.
⁶⁷ The text in the parenthesis is not part of the verse.
⁶⁸ Yeshayahu 40:9.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:28:1)


**אֲשֶׁר עוֹד בִּקְשָׁה נַפְשִׁי.** לְבַד אֵלֶּה הָאֲמוּרִים לְמַעְלָה שֶׁבִּקַּשְׁתִּים וְלֹא מְצָאתִים, בִּקְשָׁה עוֹד נַפְשִׁי כְּשֵׁרָה בְנָשִׁים וְלֹא מָצָאתִי, כִּי כֻלָּן דַּעְתָּן קַלָּה עֲלֵיהֶן:


**For which yet my soul searches.** In addition to those [quests] mentioned above, which I sought and did not find, my soul sought a proper one among women, but I did not find one, because they are all lightheaded.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:28:2)


**אָדָם אֶחָד מֵאֶלֶף מָצָאתִי.** בְּנוֹהֵג שֶׁבָּעוֹלָם, אֶלֶף נִכְנָסִים לְמִקְרָא, אֵין יוֹצְאִים מֵהֶם לְהַצְלִיחַ שֶׁרְאוּיִם לְמִשְׁנָה אֶלָּא מֵאָה, וְאוֹתָם מֵאָה שֶׁנִּכְנְסוּ לְמִשְׁנָה, אֵין יוֹצְאִים מֵהֶם לִגְמָרָא אֶלָּא עֲשָׂרָה, וְאוֹתָן עֲשָׂרָה שֶׁנִּכְנָסִין לִגְמָרָא, אֵין מַצְלִיחַ מֵהֶם אֶלָּא אֶחָד לְהוֹרָאָה, הֲרֵי אֶחָד מֵאֶלֶף:


**One [worthy] man among a thousand I have found.** It is customary in the world, [that out of] a thousand who enter [a school] to [learn] Scripture, emerge from them— successful and capable of [the study of] Mishnah— only one hundred. And of those hundred who enter [to study] Mishnah, only ten of them emerge for [the study of] Gemara, and of those ten who enter to [study] Gemara, only one emerges who is capable of rendering decisions [pertaining to religious matters], the result being one out of a thousand.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:28:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:28:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:28:3)


**וְאִשָּׁה בְכָל אֵלֶּה.** אֲפִלּוּ בְאֶלֶף, לְכַךְ אַתָּה צָרִיךְ לְהִזָּהֵר בָּהּ:


**But a [worthy] woman among all of them.** Even in a thousand; therefore, you must be cautious regarding her.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:29:1)


**לְבַד רְאֵה זֶה מָצָאתִי.** שֶׁבָּאָה לְעוֹלָם תַּקָּלָה עַל יָדָהּ:


**See, this only have I found.** And which brought to the world a stumbling block through her.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:29:2)


**אֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה.** הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא אֶת הָאָדָם הָרִאשׁוֹן יָשָׁר:


**That [God] made.** The Holy One, Blessed Is He [created] Adam perfectly upright.⁶⁹

footnotes:
⁶⁹ As God stated in Bereishis 3:22 regarding Adam, “Behold, the man has become like one of us to know [what is] good and [what is] evil.”


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:29:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:29:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:29:3)


**וְהֵמָּה.** מִּשֶּׁנִּזְדַּוְּגָה לוֹ חַוָּה אִשְׁתּוֹ וְנַעֲשׂוּ שְׁנַיִם וְנִקְרְאוּ "הֵמָּה":


**But they.** When his wife Chavah was paired up with him,⁷⁰ and they became two and were called “they.”

footnotes:
⁷⁰ Rashi is explaining the reason for the change to plural [“they”] when the subject is singular [“man”].


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 7:29:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:29:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/7:29:4)


**בִּקְשׁוּ חִשְּׁבֹנוֹת רַבִּים.** מְזִמּוֹת וּמַחֲשָׁבוֹת שֶׁל חֵטְא. כַּךְ נִדְרָשׁ בַּמִּדְרָשׁ:


**Sought out many stratagems.** Plans and designs of sin. So is it expounded in the Midrash.



## Chapter 8


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:1:1)


**מִי כְּהֶחָכָם.** מִי בָעוֹלָם חָשׁוּב כְּאָדָם חָכָם?


**Who is like the wise person.** Who in the world is as important as a wise man?


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:1:2)


**וּמִי יוֹדֵעַ פֵּשֶׁר דָּבָר.** פִּשְׁרוֹן שֶׁל דָּבָר. כְּמוֹ שֶׁמָּצִינוּ בְדָנִיֵּאל, מִתּוֹךְ חָכְמָתוֹ, שֶׁהָיָה חָכָם בְּיִרְאַת שָׁמַיִם, נִתְגַּלּוּ לוֹ רָזֵי פִשְׁרִין. מִי כְמשֶׁה, עוֹשֶׂה פְשָׁרִים בֵּין יִשְׂרָאֵל לַאֲבִיהֶם שֶׁבַּשָּׁמָיִם?


**And who knows how to interpret things.** [I.e.,] the solution פִּשְׁרוֹן of things,¹ as we find regarding Daniyeil, because of his wisdom, for he was wise in the fear of Heaven, secret solutions פִּשְׁרוֹן were revealed to him. Who is like Moshe, who brought about compromises between Yisroel and their Father in Heaven?

footnotes:
¹ Alternatively, and “who is like the one who knows how to interpret things.” (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:1:3)


**וְעֹז פָּנָיו יְשֻׁנֶּא.** מִשְּׁאָר הַבְּרִיּוֹת עַד כִּי יָרְאוּ "מִגֶּשֶׁת אֵלָיו" "כִּי קָרַן עוֹר פָּנָיו":


**And the boldness of his face is changed.** From that of other people, to the extent that they feared “to approach him” [Moshe], “because the skin of his face was radiant.”² ³

footnotes:
² Shemos 34:29, 30.
³ Alternatively, wisdom begets humility and does away with anger and arrogance (Ibn Ezra). Or, because of his wisdom, everyone admires and obeys him, giving him a jubilant countenance. (Metsudas Dovid)



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:2:1)


**אֲנִי פִּי מֶלֶךְ שְׁמֹר.** לְפִיכָךְ, אֲנִי צָרִיךְ וְנָכוֹן לִשְׁמֹר פִּי מַלְכּוֹ שֶׁל עוֹלָם, שֶׁהִיא הַטּוֹבָה שֶׁבְּכֻלָּן:


**My [advice to you is] keep the King’s command.** Therefore, I need to and I am prepared to observe the commandment of the King of the Universe, which is the best of them all.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:2:2)


**וְעַל דִּבְרַת שְׁבוּעַת אֱלֹהִים.** שֶׁנִּשְׁבַּעְנוּ לוֹ בְחוֹרֵב לִשְׁמֹר מִצְוֹתָיו.


**And do so because of the oath to God.** Which we swore to Him at Choreiv, to keep His commandments.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:2:3)


דָּבָר אַחֵר: אֲנִי נָכוֹן לִשְׁמֹר פִּי מַלְכֵי הָאֻמּוֹת הַמּוֹשְׁלִים בָּנוּ בְּגֻלְגָלִיּוֹת וְאַרְנוּנִיּוֹת. **וְעַל דִּבְרַת שְׁבוּעַת אֱלֹהִים.** וּבִלְבַד שֶׁלֹּא יַעֲבִירֵנוּ אֶת הַשְּׁבוּעָה שֶׁנִּשְׁבַּעְנוּ לַמָּקוֹם.


Another explanation: I am prepared to observe the command of the kings of the nations who rule over us with head taxes and property taxes. **And do so because of the oath to God.** Provided that they do not cause us to violate the oath [of allegiance] that we swore to the Omnipresent.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:2:4)


**וְעַל דִּבְרַת.** וְאֵצֶל דִּבְרַת שְׁבוּעַת אֱלֹהִים אֶשְׁמֹר פִּי הַמְּלָכִים. וְכֵן מָצִינוּ בַחֲנַנְיָה, מִישָׁאֵל וַעֲזַרְיָה, שֶׁאָמְרוּ לִנְבוּכַדְנֶצַּר, "וְאָמְרִין לְמַלְכָּא, נְבוּכַדְנֶאצַּר לָא חַשְׁחִין אֲנַחְנָא עַל דְּנָּה פִּתְגָם לַהֲתָבוּתָךְ". אִם מַלְכָּא, לָמָה נְבוּכַדְנֶאצַּר? וְאִם נְבוּכַדְנֶאצַּר, לָמָּה מַלְכָּא? אֶלָּא כַּךְ אָמְרוּ לוֹ: "לָךְ אָנוּ אוֹמְרִים דְּאַתְּ מַלְכָּא לָנוּ לַעֲבוֹדָה וּלְמִסִּים לְגֻלְגָּלִיּוֹת, אַךְ עַל דְּנָא שֶׁאַתְּ אוֹמֵר עֲלָנָא לַעֲבֹד עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה נְבוּכַדְנֶאצַּר אַתְּ, וְלֹא מֶלֶךְ":


**And do so because of [the oath].** And alongside the oath of God, I will keep the command of the kings.⁴ And similarly we find with Chananyah, Mishaeil, and Azaryah, who said to Nevuchadnetzar, “they said, ‘O King Nevuchadnetzar, we are not worried about replying to you about this matter.’”⁵ If he was king why [call him] “Nevuchadnetzar,” and if Nevuchadnetzar, why [call him] “king”? But, this is what they said to him, “We say to you that you are our ‘king’ regarding work and the head taxes, but regarding your ordering us to worship idols, you are ‘Nevuchadnetzar,’ and not king.”

footnotes:
⁴ I.e., I will obey the command of kings as long as it does not conflict with the oath of allegiance that we swore to God.
⁵ Daniyeil 3:16.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:3:1)


**אַל תִּבָּהֵל מִפָּנָיו תֵּלֵךְ.** אַל תְּהִי נִבְהָל לוֹמַר שֶׁתֵּלֵךְ וְתִבְרַח מִפָּנָיו לְמָקוֹם שֶׁאֵינוֹ שַׁלִּיט שָׁם, שֶׁבְּכָל מָקוֹם הוּא שַׁלִּיט:


**Do not hasten to leave His Presence.** Do not be hasty and say that you intend to flee from His Presence to a place where He does not reign, for He reigns everywhere.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:3:2)


**אַל תַּעֲמֹד בְּדָבָר רָע.** אַל תַּעֲמִיד עַצְמְךָ עוֹסֵק בִּדְבָרִים רָעִים:


**Do not persist in doing evil.** Do not persist to engage in evil matters.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Alternatively, “do not pause at a bad thing,” i.e., an inauspicious verse. When reading from the Torah, the reader should begin and/or end at a good matter, e.g., he may stop in the middle of section dealing with blessings. However, he may not stop in the middle of the section dealing with curses, but he must read them all without interruption. (Midrash)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:3:3)


**כִּי כָּל אֲשֶׁר יַחְפֹּץ.** לְהִפָּרַע מִמְּךָ, רְשׁוּת וִיכֹלֶת בְּיָדוֹ לַעֲשׂוֹת:


**For whatever pleases Him.** To mete retribution upon you, He has the authority and ability to do so.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:4:1)


**בַּאֲשֶׁר דְּבַר מֶלֶךְ שִׁלְטוֹן.** בִּשְׁבִיל אֲשֶׁר דְּבַר הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא מוֹשֵׁל הוּא, וּמִי יֹאמַר לוֹ מַה תַּעֲשֶׂה. וְאִם שׁוֹמֵר מִצְוָה אַתָּה, לֹא תֵדַע דָּבָר רָע וְלֹא תְבוֹאֶךָ:


**For the King’s word is all powerful.** Because the word of the Holy One, Blessed Is He is the Ruler, and who will say to Him, “What are You doing?” But if you “preserve the commandment,”⁷ you will know no evil and it will not befall you.⁸

footnotes:
⁷ Below, verse 5.
⁸ Alternatively, verses 3-4 refer to a mortal king and are translated accordingly. (Metsudas Dovid)



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:5:1)


**וְעֵת וּמִשְׁפָּט יֵדַּע לֵב חָכָם.** הֶחָכָם יוֹדֵעַ שֶׁיֵשׁ עֵת קָבוּעַ לִפְקוּדַּת רְשָׁעִים, וּמִשְׁפָּטִים יֵשׁ לִפְנֵי הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא שֶׁסּוֹפוֹ לְהִפָּרַע מֵהֶם. מִשְׁפָּט יוסטיצ"א בְּלַעַ"ז, וְהִיא הַפֻּרְעָנוּת:


**And [the significance of] time and judgment is known by the wise man.** The wise person perceives that there is a predetermined time for the visitation of punishment upon the wicked, and there are judgments before the Holy One, Blessed Is He, with which He will exact justice from them. Judgment is *justice* in O.F., and it refers to the punishment.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:6:1)


**כִּי לְכָל חֵפֶץ.** שֶׁהָאָדָם עוֹשֶׂה חֶפְצוֹ וְעוֹבֵר עַל דָּת, יֵשׁ עֵת לְהִפָּרַע, וּמִשְׁפָּט וּפֻרְעָנוּת מוּכֶנֶת:


**For every matter.** When a person satisfies his desire and transgresses the law, a time to exact retribution [is cast], and justice and punishment are ready.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:6:2)


**כִּי רָעַת הָאָדָם רַבָּה עָלָיו.** כַּאֲשֶׁר רַבָּה רָעַת הָאָדָם וּסְאָתוֹ גְדוּשָׁה, אָז בָּאָה פְקֻדָּתוֹ, וְ"כִי" מְשַׁמֵּשׁ בִּלְשׁוֹן "כַּאֲשֶׁר", כְּמוֹ, "כִּי יִהְיֶה לָהֶם דָּבָר בָּא אֵלַי":


**When the evil of man overwhelms him.** When man’s evil becomes exceedingly overwhelming and his measure overflows, then his punishment arrives; ‘וְכִי’ is used as an expression of “when,” as in, “When כִּי they have a concern, it comes to me.”⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Shemos 18:16.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:7:1)


**כִּי אֵינֶנּוּ יֹדֵעַ מָה שֶׁיִּהְיֶה.** כְּשֶׁהָרָשָׁע עוֹבֵר עֲבֵרָה, אֵינֶנּוּ נוֹתֵן לִבּוֹ לְמַה שֶׁעָתִיד הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא לַהֲבִיאוֹ בְמִשְׁפָּט, וְאוֹי לוֹ בְכַךְ:


**For he knows not what will be.** When the wicked man sins, he does not give thought that the Holy One, Blessed Is He, is destined to bring him to judgment, and woe is to him because of this.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ Alternatively, man is unable to foresee specific future events. Ibn Ezra


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:7:2)


**כִּי כַּאֲשֶׁר יִהְיֶה.** הַפֻּרְעָנוּת, מִי יַגִּיד לוֹ לְהִמָּלֵךְ בּוֹ וְלִטֹּל בּוֹ עֵצָה וְלִטֹּל רְשׁוּת, כִּי פֶתַע פִּתְאוֹם יְבוֹאֶנּוּ:


**For when it comes to pass.** The punishment, “who will tell him,” [i.e.,] to discuss [the matter] with him and to take advice and to ask permission, for it will suddenly befall him.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ Alternatively, when a person is about to sin, who will forewarn him of the consequences so that he will be more cautious. (Sforno)



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:8:1)


**אֵין אָדָם שַׁלִּיט בָּרוּחַ.** בְּרוּחוֹ וְיִצְרוֹ שֶׁל שְׁלוּחוֹ שֶׁל מָקוֹם, לִכְלוֹא וְלִמְנֹעַ מִמֶּנּוּ אֶת הָרוּחַ שֶׁבְּגוּפוֹ שֶׁלֹּא יִטְּלֶנּוּ מַלְאַךְ הַמָּוֶת:


**Man has no power over the spirit.** [I.e., over] the spirit and inclination of the emissary¹² of the Omnipresent, to retain and withhold from him the spirit in his body, so that the angel of death should not take it.

footnotes:
¹² I.e., the angel of death. We find the term רוח [=spirit] referring to angels in Tehillim 104:4, “He makes the רוחות His messengers.”


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:8:2)


**וְאֵין שִׁלְטוֹן.** שֶׁל שׁוּם מֶלֶךְ נִיכָּר בְּיוֹם מוֹתוֹ. בְּכָל מָקוֹם אַתָּה מוֹצֵא "וְהַמֶּלֶךְ דָּוִד", וּבְיוֹם מוֹתוֹ "וַיִּקְרְבוּ יְמֵי דָוִד לָמוּת", וְלֹא הֻזְכַּר הַמַּלְכוּת כַּאן:


**He has no power.** [The power] of any king [is not] discernable on the day of his death.¹³ Everywhere [in Scripture] you find [that Dovid is referred to as], “King Dovid,” but on the day of his death [it states], “And the days of Dovid drew near that he should die,”¹⁴ and no kingship is mentioned here.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹³ Alternatively, all the power of the ruler is thwarted on the day of his death, i.e., to save himself from death.
¹⁴ I Melochim 2:1.
¹⁵ Though in Shemos 2:23 it states “the king of Egypt died,” that is speaking of a historic event that happened in the past, however, when Scripture speaks of the event as it is occurring, kingship is not mentioned. (Likutei Yehudah)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:8:3)


**וְאֵין מִשְׁלַחַת בַּמִּלְחָמָה.** זוּ לֵאמֹר: "אֶשְׁלַח בְּנִי אוֹ עַבְדִּי בִמְקוֹמִי:


**Nor dispatch in battle.** [In] this [battle by] saying, “I will send my son or my servant instead of me.”¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ Rashi interprets משחת as dispatch or send [שלוח]. Alternatively, משחת means weaponry, as in II Divrei Hayomim 32:5 and Nechemyah 4:11. Accordingly, this phrase means that weaponry [prepared for war] is of no avail to save one who is destined to die.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:9:1)


**אֶת כָּל זֶה רָאִיתִי.** הָאָמוּר לְמָעְלָה:


**All this I have seen.** What is mentioned above.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:9:2)


**וְנָתוֹן אֶת לִבִּי לְכָל מַעֲשֶׂה.** וְגַם לְכָל מַעֲשֵׂה בְנֵי אָדָם נָתַתִּי אֶת לִבִּי, וְרָאִיתִי עֵת אֲשֶׁר שָׁלַט אָדָם בַּחֲבֵרוֹ וְגָבַר עָלָיו, וְסוֹפוֹ נֶהְפַּךְ לְרָעָתוֹ. עֲמָלֵק נִתְגַּבֵּר עַל יִשְׂרָאֵל, וְסוֹפוֹ "וְאַחֲרִיתוֹ עֲדֵי אֹבֵד". וְכֵן פַּרְעֹה, וְכֵן נְבוּכַדְנֶאצַּר, וְכֵן סַנְחֵרִיב:


**And I applied my heart to [understand] all the work.** And also to all mankind’s work I applied my mind, and I saw the time that a man ruled over another and overpowered him, and ultimately it turned to his own detriment.¹⁷ [E.g.,] Amoleik overpowered Yisroel, and ultimately [it states about Amoleik], “and his end will be that he will be lost forever.”¹⁸ And the same happened to Pharaoh, and the same with Nevuchadnetzar, and the same with Sancheiriv.

footnotes:
¹⁷ Because, “God seeks the pursued,” see above 3:15.
¹⁸ Bamidbar 24:20.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:10:1)


**וּבְכֵן.** וְאָז:


**And so.** And then.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:10:2)


**רָאִיתִי רְשָׁעִים קְבֻרִים.** בִּנְבוּאָה זוּ רָאִיתִי רְשָׁעִים קְבוּרִים. שֶׁהָיוּ רְאוּיִם לְהִטָּמֵן בֶּעָפָר, שֶׁהָיוּ נִבְזִים בֵּין שְׁאָר אֻמּוֹת, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר עֲלֵיהֶם "זֶה הָעָם לֹא הָיָה". וְשָׁלְטוּ בְּבֵיתוֹ שֶׁל הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא שֶׁהוּא מָקוֹם קָדוֹשׁ, וּבְלֶכְתָּם מִשָּׁם אֶל אַרְצָם, הָיוּ מִשְׁתַּבְּחִים בְּעִירָם אֲשֶׁר כַּךְ וְכַךְ עָשׂוּ בְּבֵיתוֹ מָקוֹם. אַל תִּקְרֵי "וְיִשְׁתַּכְּחוּ", אֶלָּא "וְיִשְׁתַּבְּחוּ". כַּךְ דְּרָשׁוּהוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה, וּלְעִנְיַן הַשִׁכְחָה כַּךְ נִדְרָשׁ בָּאַגָּדָה: וְסוֹפָם שֶׁיִּשְׁתַּכַּח שְׁמָם וְזִכְרָם מִן הָעִיר עַצְמָהּ אֲשֶׁר כֵּן עָשׂוּ בָהּ, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר "וְקִבַּצְּתִּי אֶת כָּל הַגּוֹיִם. . . אֶל עֵמֶק יְהוֹשָׁפָט". בִּמְקוֹם שֶׁנִּאֲצוּ לְפָנָיו, יִפָּרַע מֵהֶם, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר "ה' בָּעִיר צַלְמָם תִּבְזֶה":


**I have seen how the wicked were buried.** In this prophecy, I saw wicked men buried, who were worthy of being buried in the dust, for they were held in contempt by the nations, about whom it is stated, “this people has never been,”¹⁹ and they ruled over the Beis [Hamikdosh] of the Holy One, Blessed Is He, which is a holy place. And when they went from there to their land, they boasted in their city that they did such and such in the Beis [Hamikdosh] of the Omnipresent. Do not read ‘וְיִשְׁתַּכְּחוּ’, [=and they will be forgotten,] but ‘וְיִשְׁתַּבְּחוּ’, [=and they will boast]. So did our Rabbis of blessed memory expound it.²⁰ Concerning the forgetting,²¹ it is expounded as follows in the Aggadah: And ultimately, their name and their remembrance will be forgotten from that very city, that they did so therein, as it is stated, “I will gather all the nations [and I will take them down] to the Valley of Yehoshophot.”²² In the place where they angered Him, He will mete out retribution upon them, and so it states, “O Adonoy, in the city, You will despise their form.”²³

footnotes:
¹⁹ Yeshayahu 23:13.
²⁰ See Maseches Gittin 56b.
²¹ I.e., the wording of the written text [וישתכחו].
²² Yoel 4:2.
²³ Tehillim 73:20.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:10:3)


**גַּם זֶה.** אֶחָד מִן הַהֲבָלִים שֶׁנִּמְסְרוּ לְעוֹלָם לְיַגֵּעַ אֶת הַבְּרִיּוֹת, שֶׁאֵין הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא מְמַהֵר לְהִפָּרַע מֵעוֹשֵׂי הָרָעָה, וְהַבְּרִיּוֹת סוֹבְרִים, אֵין דִּין וְאֵין דַיָּן:


**This too.** Is one of the vanities that were given to the world to weary mankind, for the Holy One, Blessed Is He, does not rush to mete out retribution upon evildoers, and [therefore] mankind thinks that there is neither judgment nor Judge.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:11:1)


**אֲשֶׁר אֵין נַעֲשָׂה פִתְגָם.** מִשְׁפַּט מַעֲשֵׂה הָרָעָה מְהֵרָה, שֶׁאֵין הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא מְמַהֵר לְהִפָּרַע מֵעוֹשֵׂי הָרָעָה, וְעַל כֵּן הֵם סוֹבְרִים אֵין דִּין, וּמָלֵא לִבָּם בְּקִרְבָּם לַעֲשׂוֹת רָע:


**Because the execution of sentence.** Punishment for the evil deeds [is not done] quickly. For the Holy One, Blessed Is He, does not rush to mete out retribution upon the evildoers, and therefore, they think that there is no judgment, and they are encouraged to do evil.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:12:1)


**אֲשֶׁר חֹטֶא וְגוֹ'.** לְפִי שֶׁרוֹאִים שֶׁהַחוֹטֵא עוֹשֶׂה רָע מְאַת אֲלָפִים וְרִבּוֹאוֹת וּמַאֲרִיךְ לוֹ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא, וְאֵינוֹ נִפְרָע מִמֶּנּוּ:


**Though a sinner, etc.** Because they see that the sinner does evil a hundred thousand and myriads [of times], and the Holy One, Blessed Is He, grants him an extension without meting out retribution.²⁴

footnotes:
²⁴ As Iyov [24:12] complained, “From the city people groan, and the souls of the slain cry out; yet God does not impute it for unseemliness.” (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:12:2)


**מְאַת.** מִקְרָא קָצֵר, וְדָבוּק לְפָנָיו, לוֹמַר מְאַת יָמִים, מְאַת שָׁנִים, מְאַת אֶלֶף, וְכֵן "וּשְׁכֻרַת". מִקְרָא קָצֵר, וְחָסֵר שְׁכֻרַת כַּעַס, וְלֹא מִיָּיִן כִּשְׁאָר שִׁכְרוּת:


**A hundred times.** This is an ellipsis,²⁵ and it is connected to [the words] preceding it, meaning, a hundred days, a hundred years, a hundred thousand. And similarly, [what Scripture states], “and drunk וּשְׁכֻרַת,”²⁶ is an ellipsis, and it is missing drunk “from wrath” but not from wine, like other drunkenness.

footnotes:
²⁵ I.e., the omission of a word or words necessary for complete construction but understood in the context.
²⁶ Yeshayahu 51:21.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:12:3)


**כִּי גַּם יוֹדֵעַ אָנִי.** כִּי אַף בְּכָל זֹאת, שֶׁאֵינוֹ מְמַהֵר לְהִפָּרַע מִן הָרְשָׁעִים וְלִהְיוֹת הֶפְרֵשׁ בֵּין צַדִּיקִים לִרְשָׁעִים, יוֹדֵעַ אֲנִי שֶׁסוֹף כָּל אֶחָד וְאֶחָד לִטֹּל שְׂכָרוֹ, וְלִירֵאָיו יִהְיֶה טוֹב:


**Nevertheless I know.** For despite all this, that He does not rush to mete retribution upon the wicked to make a distinction, between the righteous and the wicked, I know that each one will ultimately receive his due, and that those who fear Him will fare well.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:13:1)


**וְטוֹב לֹא יִהְיֶה לְרָשָׁע.** לְפִי אֲשֶׁר אֵינֶנּוּ יָרֵא לִפְנֵי הָאֱלֹהִים:


**And it will not be well with the wicked.** Because he does not fear God.²⁷

footnotes:
²⁷ Based on this verse, the Gemara in Kiddushin 33b states that any scholar who does not rise before his teacher is considered wicked, will not live long and will forget his learning.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:14:1)


**יֵשׁ הֶבֶל.** דָּבָר הַמַּהֲבִיל אֶת הַבְּרִיּוֹת:


**There is a vanity.** Something that confounds mankind.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:14:2)


**אֲשֶׁר יֵשׁ צַדִּיקִים.** שֶׁמַּגִּיעַ אֲלֵיהֶם רָעָה כְּמַעֲשֵׂה הָרְשָׁעִים וְיֵשׁ רְשָׁעִים שֶׁמַּגִּיעַ אֲלֵיהֶם טוֹבָה כְּמַעֲשֵׂה הַצַּדִיקִים. אָמַרְתִּי שֶׁגַם זֶה אֶחָד מִן הַהֲבָלִים הַנּוֹהֲגִים בָּעוֹלָם. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ דָרְשׁוּ לְצַד אַחֵר בְּמַסֶּכֶת הוֹרָיוֹת, וְאֵינוֹ מְיֻשָּׁב לִי עַל שִׁיטַת דִּבּוּר שֶׁסִיֵּם הֶחָכָם לוֹמַר שֶׁגַּם זֶה הָבֶל:


**There are righteous men.** To whom evil happens [as though] they acted wickedly, and there are wicked to whom good happens [as though] they acted righteously.²⁸ I said that this too is one of the vanities that prevails in the world. However, our Rabbis expounded it in a different manner in Maseches Horiyos,²⁹ but it is not explained satisfactorily to me, according to the words that the wise man concludes by saying, “This too is vanity.”

footnotes:
