²⁸ Alternatively, there are righteous men who acquire a bad reputation as though they acted wickedly, is referring to a foolish righteous person who takes the practices of his piety to ridiculous levels (Sforno). Mesilas Yeshorim states that one must not decide upon the saintliness of a deed on the basis of its surface appearance alone, rather he should view the deed, including its consequence(s), from every angle of human intelligence, and then determine whether or not to perform the deed.
²⁹ 10b.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:15:1)


**אֶת הַשִּׂמְחָה.** שֶׁיְּהֵא שָׂמֵחַ בְּחֶלְקוֹ וְעוֹסֵק בִּפְקוּדִים יְשָׁרִים מְשַׂמְּחֵי לֵב, וְלֹא יְהֵא שָׁטוּף אַחַר הַרְבּוֹת הוֹן בְּנֶשֶׁךְ וּבְמַרְבִּית וְגָזֵל. כָּל מִי שֶׁאֵינוֹ שָׂמֵחַ בְּחֶלְקוֹ וְשָׁטוּף אַחַר הַמָּמוֹן, בָּא לִידֵי עֲבֵרוֹת גָּזֵל וְאוֹנָאָה וְרִבִּית, וְשֶׁאֵינוֹ שָׂמֵחַ בְּחֶלְקוֹ לְעִנְיַן אַהֲבַת אִשְׁתּוֹ, שָׁטוּף אַחֲרֵי הַנָּשִׁים לְהַרְהֵר אַחַר אֵשֶׁת אִישׁ:


**Rejoicing.** That he should be happy with in his lot³⁰ and engage in the “upright commands,” which “cause the heart to rejoice,”³¹ and he should not be immersed in increasing his wealth by means of usury, interest and robbery.³² Whoever is not happy with his lot and is immersed in amassing wealth, eventually commits the sins of robbery, fraud, and usury. Also, one who is not happy with his lot concerning the love of his wife, is immersed in [a passionate lust for] women, and harbors sinful thoughts about married women.

footnotes:
³⁰ Alternatively, I extolled the virtuous trait of rejoicing, i.e., the trait of being able to accept every occurrence in life with happiness and joy. (Derech Hachayim)
³¹ Tehillim 19:9.
³² The Gemara in Maseches Shabbos 30b sees an apparent contradiction between this verse which extolls the concept of rejoicing, and the verse above [2:2], which states, “and of joy, what does it accomplish.” The Gemara explains that the verse here is speaking of a celebration or rejoicing of a mitzvah, e.g., a marriage, therefore rejoicing is extolled, whereas verse 2:2 above speaks of rejoicing without purpose, and is therefore frowned upon.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:15:2)


**לֶאֱכֹל וְלִשְׁתּוֹת.** מִמַּה שֶׁחָנַן לוֹ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא וְלִשְׂמֹחַ בְּחֶלְקוֹ. וּמִדְרַשׁ אֲגָדָה: כָּל אֲכִילָה וּשְׁתִיָּה שֶׁבְּקֹּהֶלֶת אֵינָהּ אֶלָּא תַּלְמוּד תּוֹרָה, כָּעִנְיָן שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר "לְכוּ שִׁבְרוּ וֶאֱכֹלוּ וְגוֹ'":


**To eat and drink.** From that which the Holy One, Blessed Is He, graciously granted him, and to be happy with his lot. And the Midrash Aggadah [states], that all mention of “eating and drinking” in Koheles, refers only to the study of Torah, as the matter is stated, “go, buy, and eat, etc.”³³

footnotes:
³³ Yeshayahu 55:1.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:15:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:15:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:15:3)


**וְהוּא יִלְוֶנּוּ.** יִתְחַבֵּר עִמּוֹ, כָּעִנְיָן שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר "וְהָלַךְ לְפָנֶיךָ צִדְקֶךָ":


**And this will accompany him.** Will join him, as the matter is stated, “and your righteousness shall go before you.”³⁴

footnotes:
³⁴ Ibid. 58:8.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:15:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:15:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:15:4)


**יְמֵי חַיָּיו אֲשֶׁר נָתַן לוֹ הָאֱלֹהִים.** יַעֲשֶׂה כַּךְ, וְסוֹף הַמִּקְרָא מוּסָב עַל רֹאשׁוֹ, וּמִקְרָא מְסֹרָס הוּא: אֵין טוֹב לָאָדָם יְמֵי חַיָּיו אֲשֶׁר נָתַן לוֹ הָאֱלֹהִים כִּי אִם לֶאֱכֹל וְלִשְׁתּוֹת וְלִשְׂמֹחַ, וְהוּא יִלְוֶנּוּ בַעֲמָלוֹ:


**[All] the days of his life which God has given him.** He shall do so, and the end of the verse is connected to its beginning, and it is a transposed verse [whose meaning is], “there is nothing better for man in [all] the days of his life that God has given him than to eat and drink and be joyful, and this will accompany him in his toil.”



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:16:1)


**כַּאֲשֶׁר נָתַתִּי אֶת לִבִּי.** אֵין "כַּאֲשֶׁר" זֶה מְשַׁמֵּשׁ לְשׁוֹן דֻּגְמָא, כְּמוֹ "כַּאֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה כֵּן יֵעָשֶׂה", אֶלָּא לְשׁוֹן זְמַן, כְּמוֹ "כַּאֲשֶׁר בָּא יוֹסֵף", "כַּאֲשֶׁר כִּלּוּ לֶאֱכֹל וְגוֹ'", אַף זֶה כֵן, כְּשֶׁהָיִיתִי נוֹתֵן לִבִּי "לָדַעַת וְלָתוּר, וְגוֹ'," "אָז וְרָאִיתִי אֶת כָּל מַעֲשֵׂה הָאֱלֹהִים":


**When I applied my heart.** This “כַּאֲשֶׁר” is not used as an expression of an example, as in, “as כַּאֲשֶׁר he did, so shall it be done,”³⁵ rather it is an expression of time, as in, “When כַּאֲשֶׁר Yoseif came,”³⁶ [and as in,] “when כַּאֲשֶׁר they finished eating, etc.”³⁷ This too is so, When כַּאֲשֶׁר I applied my heart “to know, to explore, etc.,”³⁸ then, “I saw all the works of God.”

footnotes:
³⁵ Vayikra 24:19.
³⁶ Bereishis 37:23.
³⁷ Ibid. 43:2.
³⁸ Above 7:25.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:16:2)


**שֵׁנָה בְּעֵינָיו אֵינֶנּוּ רֹאֶה.** הַרָשָׁע הַשָּׁטוּף אַחַר הַמָּמוֹן וְהָעֲרָיוֹת:


**[Neither] does he see sleep with his eyes.** The wicked person³⁹ who has a passionate lust for money and for forbidden women.⁴⁰

footnotes:
³⁹ According to Rashi, the subject of the phrase, “neither does he sleep...,” is the wicked person, who does not sleep because he is so engrossed in his activities. Alternatively, שנה [=sleep] can also be a reference to death, as in Daniyeil 12:2, “many of those who sleep [מישני] in the dusty earth [i.e., the dead] will awaken.” The wicked person never considers his death, and that ultimately, he will have to give a reckoning for his activities, and therefore he does not repent.
⁴⁰ Alternatively, the subject of this phrase is Koheles, who says that he was so engrossed in his study “to know wisdom and to observe etc.” that sleep eluded him. (Ibn Ezra)



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:17:1)


**וְרָאִיתִי אֶת כָּל מַעֲשֵׂה הָאֱלֹהִים.** שֶׁמָּסַר לַבְּרִיּוֹת:


**And I saw all the works of God.** That He gave over to mankind.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:17:2)


**כִּי לֹא יוּכַל הָאָדָם לִמְצוֹא וְגוֹ'.** אֵין הַבְּרִיּוֹת יְכוֹלִין לַעֲמוֹד עַל סוֹף דְּרָכָיו שֶׁל הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא מַה שְׂכַר כָּל הַמַּעֲשֶׂה שֶׁנַּעֲשָׂה תַחַת הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ, שֶׁרוֹאִין רְשָׁעִים מַצְלִיחִים וְצַדִּיקִים יוֹרְדִים:


**That man cannot discover, etc.** Mankind is unable to fathom the ways of the Holy One, Blessed Is He, what the reward is for all the work that is done under the sun, for they see wicked men prospering and righteous men declining.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:17:3)


**בְּשֶׁל אֲשֶׁר יַעֲמֹל הָאָדָם לְבַקֵּשׁ.** בִּשְׁבִיל אֲשֶׁר רָאִיתִי הַרְבֵּה עֲמֵלִים לְבַקֵּשׁ וְלִמְצוֹא אֶת סוֹף הַמִּדָּה וְאֵינָן יְכוֹלִין:


**And though a man labor to seek.** Because I saw many [people] toiling to seek and to fathom this matter, but they are unable.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 8:17:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:17:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/8:17:4)


**וְגַם אִם יֹאמַר הֶחָכָם.** שֶׁהוּא יוֹדֵע לֹא יוּכַל. שֶׁהֲרֵי משֶׁה רַבֵּינוּ ע"ה לֹא עָמַד עַל הַדָּבָר בְּאוֹמרוֹ "הוֹדִעֵנִי נָא אֶת דְּרָכֶךָ":


**And even though a wise man declares.** That he understands it, he will not be able to, for Moshe our teacher could not comprehend this matter,⁴¹ when he said, “please make Your way known to me.”⁴²

footnotes:
⁴¹ Even a wise man is unable to fathom the ways of God. (Metsudas Dovid)
⁴² Shemos 33:13.



## Chapter 9


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:1:1)


**וְלָבוּר.** וּלְבָרֵר:


**To make [all this] clear.** And to clarify —


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:1:2)


**אֶת כָּל זֶה.** בֵּרַרְתִּי וְהִבְחַנְתִּי:


**All this.** I clarified and tested.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:1:3)


**אֲשֶׁר הַצַּדִּיקִים וְהַחֲכָמִים וַעֲבָדֵיהֶם בְּיַד הָאֱלֹהִים.** הוּא עוֹזְרָם וְהוּא שׁוֹפְטָם, כְּדֵי לְהֵיטִיבָם בְּאַחֲרִיתָם:


**That the righteous and the wise and their works are in the hand of God.** He helps them and He judges them in order to benefit them in their end.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:1:4)


**וַעֲבָדֵיהֶם.** הֵם תַּלְמִידֵיהֶם, מְשַׁמְּשֵׁיהֶם. הוֹלְכֵי אוֹרְחוֹתָם:


**And their works.** These are their students, their servants, who follow [in] their ways.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:1:5)


**גַּם אַהֲבָה גַם שִׂנְאָה.** אֵין יוֹדְעִין שְׁאָר הַבְּרִיּוֹת, וְאֵין מַכִּירִין לָתֵת לִבָּם בַּמֶּה יֵאָהֲבוּ לַמָּקוֹם וּבַמֶּה הֵם שְׂנוּאִים:


**Whether it be love or hate.** The rest of mankind neither knows nor perceives what would make them become beloved by the Omnipresent and what causes them to become hated.¹

footnotes:
¹ Alternatively, man is unable to comprehend what inspires him to love or to hate. (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:1:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:1:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:1:6)


**הַכֹּל לִפְנֵיהֶם.** לִפְנֵי הַצַּדִּיקִים וְהַחֲכָמִים:


**All is before them.** Before the righteous and the wise.²

footnotes:
² Alternatively, all preceded them, i.e., all was decreed before they were born. (Ibn Ezra)



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:1)


**הַכֹּל כַּאֲשֶׁר לַכֹּל.** הַכֹּל נוֹתְנִים לְלִבָּם כַּאֲשֶׁר מַגִּיעַ לְכָל אָדָם מִקְרֶה אֶחָד, וְיוֹדְעִים שֶׁסּוֹף דָּבָר לָתֵת לְכָל אִישׁ כְּדַרְכּוֹ:


**All things come alike to all.** All perceive when the occurrence happens to all mankind, and they know that ultimately each person is recompensed according to his ways.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:2)


**מִקְרֶה אֶחָד.** וְיוֹדְעִים שֶׁסּוֹף הַכֹּל צַדִּיק וַאֲשֶׁר רָשָׁע, לָמוּת, וּמִקְרֶה אֶחָד יֵשׁ בָּעוֹלָם הַזֶּה לְכֻלָּם. כָּל זֶה יוֹדְעִים וְאַף עַל פִּי כֵן, בּוֹחֲרִים לָהֶם דֶּרֶךְ הַטּוֹב, לְפִי שֶׁיּוֹדְעִים שֶׁיֵּשׁ הֶפְרֵשׁ בֵּינֵיהֶם לְעוֹלָם הַבָּא:


**The same fate [awaits].** And [they] know that ultimately everyone, whether righteous or wicked, will die, and they all have one fate in this world. All this they know, but nevertheless, they choose for themselves the proper path, because they know that there is a distinction between them in the World to Come.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:3)


**לַצַּדִּיק.** כְּגוֹן נֹחַ:


**The righteous.** Such as No’ach.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:4)


**וְלָרָשָׁע.** פַּרְעֹה נְכֹה, זֶה נִצְלַע וְזֶה נִצְלַע:


**And the wicked.** [Such as] Pharaoh Necho. This one became crippled, and that one [No’ach] became lame.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:5)


**לַטּוֹב.** זֶה משֶׁה:


**The good.** This is Moshe.³

footnotes:
³ Those who do good with others. (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:6)


**וְלַטָּהוֹר.** זֶה אַהֲרֹן:


**And the pure.** This is Aharon.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ Those who have pure thoughts. (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:7)


**וְלַטָּמֵא.** אֵלּוּ הַמְרַגְלִים. אֵלּוּ אָמְרוּ שִׁבְחָהּ שֶׁל אֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל, וְאֵלּוּ אָמְרוּ גְנוּתָהּ. אֵלּוּ לֹא נִכְנְסוּ לָאָרֶץ, וְאֵלּוּ לֹא נִכְנְסוּ לָאָרֶץ. הֲרֵי מִקְרֶה אֶחָד לָהֶם:


**And the impure.** These are the spies;⁵ these spoke well in praise of the land of Yisroel, but those spoke derogatorily about it. Neither these entered the land, nor those entered the land, hence, they have the same fate.

footnotes:
⁵ Those who focus on impure thoughts and levity. (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:8)


**וְלַזֹּבֵחַ.** זֶה יֹאשִׁיָּהוּ, שֶׁנֶּאֱמֶר, "וַיִּזְבַּח יֹאשִׁיָּהוּ":


**To him who brings offerings.** This is Yoshiyahu, as it is stated, “And Yoshiyahu offered a sacrifice.”⁶

footnotes:
⁶ II Divrei Hayomim 35:7. The reading of the text is, “And Yoshiyahu separated” [וירם יאשיהו] instead of “ויזבח יאשיהו.” Rashi apparently did not have our version of the text.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:9)


**וְלַאֲשֶׁר אֵינֶנּוּ זֹבֵחַ.** זֶה אַחְאָב שֶׁבִּטֵּל אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל מִלַּעֲלוֹת לְרֶגֶל. זֶה מֵת בְּחִצִּים, וְזֶה מֵת בְּחִצִּים:


**And to him who does not bring offerings.** This is Achav, who abolished [Bnei] Yisroel’s festival pilgrimages [to the Beis Hamikdosh]. and that one [Yoshiyahu] was killed by arrows.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ See I Melochim 22:34-35.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:10)


**כַּטּוֹב.** זֶה דָוִד:


**As is with the good man.** This is Dovid.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:11)


**כַּחֹטֶא.** זֶה נְבוּכַדְנֶאצַּר. זֶה בָנָה בֵית הַמִּקְדָּשׁ, וְזֶה הֶחֱרִיבוֹ. זֶה מָלַךְ מ' שָׁנָה, וְזֶה מָלַךְ מ' שָׁנָה:


**So is it with the sinner.** This is Nevuchadnetzar. This one [Dovid] built the Beis Hamikdosh, and that one destroyed it; this one reigned forty years, and that one reigned forty years.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:12)


**הַנִּשְׁבָּע.** זֶה צִדְקִיָּהוּ שֶׁנִּשְׁבַּע לַשֶּׁקֶר, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר, "וְגַם נְבוּכַדְנֶאצַּר מָרָד אֲשֶׁר הִשְׁבִּיעוֹ וְגוֹ':


**As is with one who swears [easily].** This is Tzidkiyahu, who swore falsely, as it is stated, “And he also rebelled against King Nevuchadnetzar, who had made him swear, etc.”⁹

footnotes:
⁹ See II Divrei Hayomim 35:23-24.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:2:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:2:13)


**כַּאֲשֶׁר שְׁבוּעָה יָרֵא.** זֶה שִׁמְשׁוֹן, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר, "וַיֹּאמֶר אֲלֵיהֶם שִׁמְשׁוֹן, הִשָּׁבְעוּ לִי פֶּן תִּפְגְעוּן בִּי אַתֶּם." לָמַדְנוּ שֶׁהָיְתָה שְׁבוּעָה חֲמוּרָה לוֹ. זֶה מֵת בְּנִקּוּר עֵינַיִם, וְזֶה מֵת בְּנִקּוּר עֵינָיִם. עַל כֵּן:


**So is it with the one who fears an oath.** This is Shimshon, as it is stated, “And Shimshon said to them, ‘Swear to me, that you, yourselves, will not strike me.’”¹⁰ We learn that he was strict in his observance of an oath. This one died after his eyes were gouged out,¹¹ and that one [Tzidkiyahu] died after his eyes were gouged out.¹² Therefore—

footnotes:
¹⁰ II Divrei Hayomim 36:13.
¹¹ Shoftim 15:12.
¹² See Shoftim 16:21.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:3:1)


**לֵב בְּנֵי אָדָם מָלֵא רָע.** שֶׁאוֹמְרִים, אֵין דִּין פֻּרְעָנוּת לָרְשָׁעִים. אֵין הַכֹּל אֶלָּא לְפִי הַמִּקְרֶה, פְּעָמִים לַצַּדִיק, וּפְעָמִים לָרָשָׁע:


**The hearts of men are filled with evil.** For they say that there is no judgment of retribution for the wicked. Everything is attributed to chance, [benefitting] sometimes the righteous, and sometimes the wicked.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:3:2)


**וְאַחֲרָיו אֶל הַמֵּתִים.** וְסוֹפָן יוֹרְדִין לְגֵיהִנֹּם:


**And afterwards they join the dead.** And they ultimately descend to Gehinnom.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:4:1)


**כִּי מִי אֲשֶׁר יְחֻבַּר אֶל כָּל הַחַיִּים, יֵשׁ בִּטָּחוֹן.** כִּי בְעוֹדוֹ בַחַיִּים, אֲפִילּוּ הוּא רָשָׁע וְנִתְחַבֵּר לָרְשָׁעִים, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶאֱמַר, "אֶל כָּל הַחַיִּים" אֲפִילּוּ לָרְשָׁעִים, יֵשׁ בִּטָּחוֹן, שֶׁמָּא יָשׁוּב לִפְנֵי מוֹתוֹ:


**For to him that is joined to all the living, there is hope.** For as long as he lives, even if he is wicked and he associates with the wicked, as it is stated, “to all the living,” [including] even the wicked, there is hope that he will repent before his death.¹³ ¹⁴

footnotes:
¹³ See II Melochim 25:7.
¹⁴ For the lowliest person, as long as he is alive, can grow spiritually, whereas the wisest and most righteous who are dead cannot. (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:4:2)


**כִּי לְכֶלֶב חַי הוּא טוֹב מִן הָאַרְיֵה הַמֵּת.** וּשְׁנֵיהֶם רְשָׁעִים. טוֹב הָיָה לוֹ לִנְבוּזַרְאֲדָּן, שֶׁהָיָה עֶבֶד רָשָׁע וְנִתְגַּיֵּר שֶׁלֹּא הִקְדִימַתּוֹ מִיתָה, מִנְּבוּכַדְנֶאצַּר רַבּוֹ שֶׁנִּקְרָא אַרְיֵה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר, "עָלָה אַרְיֵה מִסֻּבְּכוֹ". וּמֵת בְּרִשְׁעוֹ בְגֵיהִנֹּם, וְעַבְדּוֹ בְגַן עֵדֶן. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ דָרְשׁוּ לְעִנְיַן מְּחַתְּכִין אֶת הַנְּבֵלָה לִפְנֵי הַכְּלָבִים בְּשַׁבָּת, וּמֵת הַמּוּטָל בַּחַמָּה, אָסוּר לְטַלְטְלוֹ אֶלָא אִם כֵּן הֵנִיחוּ עָלָיו תִּינוֹק אוֹ כִכָּר:


**For a live dog is better than a dead lion.** And they are both wicked. It was better for Nevuzaradan, who was a wicked slave and became a proselyte, that death did not overtake him sooner than Nevuchadnetzar, his master, who is called a “lion,” as it is stated, “A lion has come up from his thicket,”¹⁵ and who died in his wickedness, in Gehinnom, while his slave [Nevuzaradan] is in the Garden of Eden. Our Rabbis expounded upon this, that we may butcher a carcass for dogs on Shabbos, but a human corpse, lying in the sun, may not be moved unless one places a child or a loaf on it.

footnotes:
¹⁵ The written text is יבחר [=will choose], to indicate that only those having the ability to choose have hope, but for the dead, being that they are unable to choose, all hope is lost. (Ibn Ezra)



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:5:1)


**כִּי הַחַיִּים יוֹדְעִים שֶׁיָּמֻתוּ.** וְאוּלַי יָשִׁיבוּ אֶל לִבָּם יוֹם הַמִּיתָה וְיָשׁוּבוּ מִדַּרְכָּם, אֲבָל מִשֶּׁמֵּתוּ, אֵינָם יוֹדְעִים מְאוּמָה, וְאֵין עוֹד לָהֶם שְׂכַר פְּעֻלָּה שֶׁיַּעֲשׂוּ מִן הַמִּיתָה וְאֵילֵךְ, אֶלָּא מִי שֶׁטָּרַח בְּעֶרֶב שַׁבָּת יֹאכַל בְּשַׁבָּת:


**For the living know that they will die.** And perhaps they will be mindful of the day of death, and they will repent of their ways, but once they die, they do not know anything,¹⁶ and they no longer earn reward for the actions¹⁷ that they do from their death and onwards, for “whoever toils on the eve of Shabbos will [have what to] eat on Shabbos.”¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁶ Yirmiyahu 4:7.
¹⁷ And even if they were to have perception, it is too late for them to repent. (Metsudas Dovid)
¹⁸ Alternatively, ואין להם עוד שכר כי נשכח זכרם means “there is no reward for them, when [= כי] their memory is forgotten,” i.e., when those who die leave no legacy of good deeds for others to follow. However, for the righteous who leave a legacy of good deeds for others to follow [i.e., their memory is not forgotten], there is further reward. (Kehilas Shlomo)



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:6:1)


**גַּם אַהֲבָתָם.** שֶׁאָהֲבוּ פֶתִי וְלָצוֹן:


**Also their love.** That they loved folly and scorn.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:6:2)


**גַּם שִׂנְאָתָם.** שֶׁשָּׂנְאוּ דָעַת:


**[Also] their hate.** That they hated knowledge.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:6:3)


**גַּם קִנְאָתָם.** שֶׁהִקְנִיאוּ לְהַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא בְּמַעֲשֵׂה יְדֵיהֶם:


**[Also] their jealousy.** That they provoked the Holy One, Blessed Is He, with their [evil] deeds. And a portion they will never again have, etc.,


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:6:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:6:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:6:4)


**וְחֵלֶק אֵין לָהֶם וְגוֹ' בְּכֹל אֲשֶׁר נַעֲשֶׂה וְגוֹ'.** לֹא הוֹעִיל לָהֶם זְכוּת בֵּן וּבַת לְאוֹתָן רְשָׁעִים שֶׁעָבְדוּ עֲבוֹדַת כּוֹכָבִים וְאֵין לָהֶם כַּפָּרָה לְאַחַר מִיתָה:


**in anything that is done, etc.** The merit of a son or a daughter did not avail those wicked men who worshiped idols, and they have no atonement after death.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:7:1)


**לֵךְ אֱכֹל בְּשִׂמְחָה.** אֲבָל אַתָּה הַצַּדִּיק, שֶׁכְּבָר רָצָה הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא מַעֲשֶׂיךָ הַטּוֹבִים וְתִזְכֶּה לָעוֹלָם הַבָּא, "לֵךְ אֱכֹל בְּשִׂמְחָה":


**Go eat [your bread] in joy.** But you, the righteous man, who the Holy One, Blessed Is He, has already accepted your good deeds and who will merit the World to Come, “go, eat [your bread] in joy.”¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁹ See Maseches Avodah Zarah 3a.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:8:1)


**בְּכָל עֵת יִהְיוּ בְגָדֶיךָ לְבָנִים.** הַתְקֵן עַצְמְךָ בְכָל שָׁעָה בְמַעֲשֶׂה טוֹב, שֶׁאִם תָּמוּת הַיּוֹם, תִּכָּנֵס בְּשָׁלוֹם. וּמָשַׁל שְׁלֹמֹה הֶחָכָם אֶת הַדָּבָר הַזֶּה לְאָדָם שֶׁהִזְמִינוֹ הַמֶּלֶךְ לְיוֹם סְעוּדָה וְלֹא קָבַע לוֹ זְמָן. אִם חָכָם הוּא אוֹ פִּקֵּחַ, מִיַּד מְכַבֵּס כְּסוּתוֹ וְרוֹחֵץ וְסָךְ, וְכֵן עֵת מָחָר, עַד עֵת יִקָּרֵא אֶל הַסְּעוּדָה יִהְיוּ כָל שָׁעָה בְגָדָיו לְבָנִים, וְהוּא רָחוּץ וְסָךְ. כַּךְ דְּרָשׁוּהוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ בְמַסֶּכֶת שַׁבָּת:


**At all times, let your garments be white.** Prepare yourself at all times with good deeds, so that if you die today, you will enter [the Hereafter] in peace. And Shlomo likened this to a man whom the king invited for a day of feast, without setting a date for him. If he is wise or clever, he will immediately launder his garments, bathe, and anoint himself.²⁰ And similarly [he will do] tomorrow until such time that he will be summoned to the feast, all this time his garments are white²¹ [=laundered] and he is bathed and anointed. So did our Rabbis expound it in Maseches Shabbos.²²

footnotes:
²⁰ Koheles addresses the righteous man to “go eat your bread in joy.” True happiness is achieved by eating “your own” bread that you have labored for, as indicated in Tehillim 128:2, “when you eat the labor of your hands you are praiseworthy and it is well with you.” Also, be satisfied with life’s basic necessities [represented by bread] without getting involved in life’s luxuries. Mesilas Yeshorim in Chapter 15 states, that once food has been swallowed, its memory is forgotten as if it had never existed, and therefore enough black bread will satiate one to the same extent as fattened swans.
²¹ According to Targum, ושמן על ראשך refers to a good reputation, i.e., do not lack a good reputation.
²² White garments were worn on festive occasions and are symbolic of purity.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:9:1)


**רְאֵה חַיִּים עִם אִשָּׁה אֲשֶׁר אָהַבְתָּ.** רְאֵה וְהָבֵן לִלְמֹד אוּמָנוּת לְהִתְפַּרְנֵס מִמֶּנּוּ, עִם תַּלְמוּד תּוֹרָה אֲשֶׁר בְיָדֶךָ:


**See [to enjoy] life with the wife you love.** See and understand to learn a skill by which to earn a livelihood,²³ together with Torah study that you possess.²⁴

footnotes:
²³ 153a.
²⁴ See Maseches Kiddushin 30b.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:9:2)


**כִּי הוּא חֶלְקְךָ בַּחַיִים.** אִם עָשִׂיתָ כֵּן, יִהְיֶה חֶלְקְךָ, זֶה חַיִּים בָּעוֹלָם הַזֶּה, לְהִתְפַּרְנֵס מִן הָאוּמָנוּת וּבָעוֹלָם הַבָּא, שֶׁיְּגִיעַת שְׁנֵיהֶם מַשְׁכַּחַת עָוֹן:


**For that is your portion in life.** If you have done so, your share will be life in this world, by earning a livelihood from the skill, and in the World to Come, because laboring in both [i.e., work and Torah study] causes sin to be forgotten.²⁵

footnotes:
²⁵ Alternatively, אשה refers to one’s soul [=נשמה]. (Alshich)



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:10:1)


**כֹּל אֲשֶׁר תִּמְצָא יָדְךָ לַעֲשׂוֹת.** רְצוֹן קוֹנְךָ בְּעוֹד שֶׁאַתָּה בְכֹחֲךָ, עֲשֵׂה:


**Whatever you are able to do.** The will of your Maker— as long as you have your strength—do.²⁶

footnotes:
²⁶ See Mishnayos Avos 2:2.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:10:2)


**כִּי אֵין מַעֲשֶׂה וְגוֹ' בִּשְׁאוֹל.** לְזַכּוֹתְךָ מִשֶּׁתָּמוּת, וְאִם עָשִׂיתָ כֵּן, אֵין לְךָ שׁוּם חֶשְׁבּוֹן בִּשְׁאוֹל שֶׁתִּדְאַג מִמֶּנּוּ. וְכֵן הַמִּקְּרָא מְסוֹרָס, כִּי אֵין מַעֲשֶׂה וְדַעַת וְחָכְמָה בִּשְׁאוֹל לָרְשָׁעִים, וְלֹא חֶשְׁבּוֹן לַצַּדִּיקִים כְּשֶׁיִּתְּנוּ הַחוֹטְאִים דִּין וְחֶשְׁבּוֹן, וְכַךְ נִדְרָשׁ בַּמִּדְרָשׁ. וְהַבָּא לְפָתְרוֹ בְלֹא סֵרוּס, כְּמַשְׁמָעוֹ, פּוֹתֵר חֶשְׁבּוֹן לְשׁוֹן מַחֲשָָׁבָה, מַה יּוּכַל עוֹד לַעֲשׂוֹת לְהִפָּטֵר מִן הַדִּין:


**For there is neither deed, etc.**, in the grave. For your merit after you die, and if you did so, you have no reckoning in the grave to worry about. The [text of the] verse is transposed [meaning], “for there is neither deed nor knowledge nor wisdom in the grave for the wicked,²⁷ nor reckoning for the righteous, when the wicked give an accounting and reckoning, and so is it expounded in the Midrash. And one who interprets it without transposing it, but according to its apparent meaning, interprets ‘חֶשְׁבּוֹן’ as an expression of “thought,” what more can he still do²⁸ to exempt himself from judgment.²⁹

footnotes:
²⁷ Alternatively, whatever charity and good deeds you are able to do, do them with all your strength. (Targum)
²⁸ For them to use as an excuse for their sins.
²⁹ I.e., once one is in the grave, there is nothing more he can think of doing to exempt himself from judgment.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:1)


**שַׁבְתִּי וְרָאֹה.** כְּמוֹ "זָכוֹר" וואדינ"ט בְּלַעַ"ז:


**Again I saw.** Like ‘זָכוֹר’[=remebering continually], *wedant* in O.F.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:2)


**לֹא לַקַּלִים הַמֵּרוֹץ.** לֹא עָמְדָה לַעֲשָׂהאֵל קַלּוּתוֹ מִשֶּׁהִגִּיעַ פְּקֻדָּתוֹ:


**The race is not to the swift.** Isohel’s swiftness³⁰ did not avail him when his time [to die] arrived.³¹

footnotes:
³⁰ See II Shmuel 2:18.
³¹ His swiftness allowed him to catch up with Avneir, who killed him. See II Shmuel 2:23.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:3)


**וְלֹא לַגִּבּוֹרִים הַמִּלְחָמָה.** לֹא עָמְדָה לְאַבְנֵר גְבוּרָתוֹ מִשֶּׁהִגִיעַ יוֹמוֹ:


**Nor the battle to the strong.** Avner’s might did not avail him when his day [to die] arrived.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:11:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:4)


**וְגַם לֹא לַחֲכָמִים לֶחֶם.** כְּגוֹן אֲנִי, שֶׁהָיָה לַחְמִי לְיוֹם, "שְׁלשִׁים כּוֹר סוֹלֶת וְגוֹמֵר" וְעַכְשָׁיו "זֶה . . . חֶלְקִי מִכָּל עֲמָלִי", מַקְלִי וּמַקֵּידָה:


**Nor does bread [come] to the wise.** For example, I, whose daily bread was “thirty kor of fine flour, etc.,”³² and now, “this [was] my portion [reward] from all my labor,”³³ my staff and my cup.

footnotes:
³² I Melochim 5:2.
³³ Above 2:10. Being wise is no guarantee for having food. Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:11:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:5)


**וְגַם לֹא לַנְבוֹנִים עשֶׁר.** כְּגוֹן אִיּוֹב, בַּתְּחִלָּה, "וַיְהִי מִקְנֵהוּ וְגוֹ'". כְּשֶׁבָּאָה שַׁעְתּוֹ, אָמַר, "חָנֻּנִי אַתֶּם רֵעָי":


**Nor riches to men of understanding.** Such as Iyov. In the beginning, “His livestock consisted of, etc.,”³⁴ But when his time came, he said, “Have pity on me, you, my friends.”³⁵

footnotes:
³⁴ Iyov 1:3.
³⁵ Ibid. 19:21. Alternatively, this phrase is connected with the preceding one. The reason that bread does not come to the wise is because wealth is not bestowed upon men of understanding, for if it were, they would share their wealth and provide the wise with bread so they would not lack anything. (Hafla’ah)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:11:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:6)


**וְגַם לֹא לַיֹּדְעִים חֵן.** הֲרֵי משֶׁה, אֵין יוֹדֵעַ וְנָבוֹן מִמֶּנּוּ בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל וְלֹא מָצָא חֵן בִּתְפִלָּתוֹ שֶׁיִּכָּנֵס לָאָרֶץ:


**Nor favor to the learned.** Referring to Moshe; there was no one more knowledgeable or wiser than he in Yisroel, and yet he did not find favor with his prayer to enter the land [of Yisroel].³⁶

footnotes:
³⁶ Alternatively, knowledge does not always earn only the acclaim of his fellow man.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:11:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:11:7)


**כִּי עֵת וָפֶגַע.** כְּמַשְׁמָעוֹ. דָּבָר אַחֵר: כִּי עֵת יִקְרֶה אוֹתָם, וְהַפְּגִיעָה וְהַתְּחִנָּה יִהְיוּ רְגִילִין בָּהֶם שֶׁלֹּא יָבוֹאוּ לַדְּבָרִים הַלָּלוּ:


**For time and chance.** [To be interpreted] as its apparent meaning.³⁷ Another explanation: “For time” will befall them, and to entreaty and supplication they should be accustomed, that these things should not befall them.

footnotes:
³⁷ Alternatively, “for time and death [=פגע] happen to them all.” (Metsudas Tzion)



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:12:1)


**כַּדָּגִים שֶׁנֶּאֱחָזִים בִּמְצוֹדָה.** כְּדָגִים גְּדוֹלִים שֶׁנֶּאֱחָזִים בִּמְצוֹדָה גְרוּעָה וַחֲלוּשָׁה וּפֵירְשׁוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ: הִיא חַכָּה, שֶׁאֵינָהּ אֶלָּא כְמִין מַחַט, וְדָג גָּדוֹל נִלְכָּד בָּהּ:


**As fish that are caught in a weak net.** Like large fish that are caught in an inferior and weak net, and our Rabbis explained: This is a fishhook, which is no more than a type of needle, by which a large fish is caught.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:12:2)


**כָּהֵם יוּקָשִׁים.** בְּמִכְשׁוֹל קָטָן וְחַלָּשׁ כְּמוֹ הַמְּצוֹדָה הָרָעָה וְהַפַּח, נִכְשָׁלִים בְּנֵי אָדָם בְּעֵת פְּקֻדַת רָעָתָם, כְּשֶׁבָא עִתָּם לִפּוֹל הָרָעָה עֲלֵיהֶם פִּתְאוֹם בִּמְצוֹדָה רָעָה, מלאוויש"ה בְלַעַ"ז:


**Like them, [men] are snared.** With a small and weak trap, like the weak net and the snare. People stumble at the time of the visitation of their evil, when their time comes for evil to fall upon them suddenly, in an inferior trap, *malweise* in O.F.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:12:3)


**כָּהֵם.** כְּמוֹתָם:


**Like them.** Like them.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:15:1)


**וְאָדָם לֹא זָכַר.** לֹא הָיָה חוֹשְׁבוֹ לִכְלוּם:


**Yet no person remembered.** No one attributed any importance to him.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:16:1)


**וְאָמַרְתִּי אָנִי.** בִּרְאוֹתִי כֵן "טוֹבָה חָכְמָה מִגְּבוּרָה". וַהֲרֵי חָכְמָתוֹ שֶׁל זֶה בְּזוּיָה לְכֻלָּם, וְעַכְשָׁיו כֻּלָּם נִמְלְטוּ עַל יָדוֹ.


**And [yet] I said.** When I saw this, “Wisdom is better than might,” for this one’s wisdom was despised by all, but now, they were all rescued through him.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:16:2)


וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה: **עִיר קְטַנָּה.** זֶה הַגּוּף.


The Midrash Aggadah [expounds as follows]: **A small city.** This is the body.³⁸

footnotes:
³⁸ See Maseches Nedarim 32b.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:16:3)


**וַאֲנָשִׁים בָּה מְעָט.** אֵלּוּ אֵבָרָיו שֶׁל אָדָם.


**With few men within it.** These are the limbs of a person.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:16:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:16:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:16:4)


**מֶלֶךְ גָּדוֹל.** זֶה יֵצֶר הָרַע, שֶׁכָּל אֵבָרָיו מַרְגִּישִׁים בּוֹ.


**A mighty king.** This is the evil inclination, which all his limbs feel.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:16:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:16:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:16:5)


**אִישׁ מִסְכֵּן.** זֶה יֵצֶר טוֹב:


**A poor [wise] man.** This is the good inclination.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:17:1)


**בְּנַחַת נִשְׁמָעִים.** מְקֻבָּלִים הֵם לַבְּרִיּוֹת:


**Are received with pleasure.** They are accepted by mankind.³⁹

footnotes:
³⁹ Alternatively, the words of the wise [spoken] gently are heard, i.e., בנחת [gently] modifies דברי [the spoken words of the wise] as opposed to בנחת נשמעים [=are received with pleasure]. (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:17:2)


**מִזַּעֲקַת מוֹשֵׁל בַּכְּסִילִים.** משֶׁה נִפְטַר זֶה כַּמָּה שָׁנִים, וַעֲדַיִּין גְזֵרוֹתָיו מְקֻבָּלִים עַל יִשְׂרָאֵל, וְכַמָּה מַלְכֵי אֻמּוֹת גּוֹזְרִין גְזֵרוֹת עַל יִשְׂרָאֵל, וְאֵין דִּבְרֵיהֶם מִתְקַיְּמִין:


**More than the shouts of a ruler of fools.** Moshe passed away many years ago, yet his decrees are still accepted by Yisroel, and how many kings of the nations issue decrees over Yisroel, but their words do not endure.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:18:1)


**טוֹבָה חָכְמָה מִכְּלֵי קְרָב.** חָכְמָתָהּ שֶׁל סֶרַח, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר, "וַתָּבוֹא הָאִשָּׁה אֶל הָעָם בְּחָכְמָתָהּ", עָמְדָה לָהֶם יוֹתֵר מִכְּלֵי קְרָב, שֶׁהָיָה בְיָדָם לְהִלָּחֵם עִם יוֹאָב:


**Wisdom is better than weapons of war.** The wisdom of Serach, of whom it is stated, “And the woman then went to [all] the people, in her wisdom,”⁴⁰ availed them more than the weapons that were in their hands, to wage war with Yo’av.⁴¹

footnotes:
⁴⁰ Rashi apparently did not have כל in his version of the text.
⁴¹ II Shmuel 20:22.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:18:2)


**וְחוֹטֶא אֶחָד יְאַבֵּד טוֹבָה הַרְבֵּה.** אִלּוּלֵי שֶׁהָרְגָה לְשֶׁבַע בֶּן בִּכְרִי, הָיוּ כֻלָּם אֲבוּדִים עַל יָדוֹ.


**But one sinner destroys a multitude of good.** Had she not slain Sheva the son of Bichri, they would all have been destroyed by him.⁴²

footnotes:
⁴² Alternatively, the wisdom of Yaakov was more effective than Eisav’s weapons. (Midrash)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 9:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/9:18:3)


דָּבָר אַחֵר: **וְחוֹטֶא אֶחָד יְאַבֵּד טוֹבָה הַרְבֵּה.** הֲרֵי שֶׁהָיוּ יִשְׂרָאֵל מֶחֱצָה צַדִּיקִים וּמֶחֱצָה רְשָׁעִים, וּבָא אֶחָד וְחָטָא וַעֲשָׂאָן מְרֻבִּים, נִמְצָא שֶׁהִכְרִיעַ אֶת כֻּלָּם לְחוֹבָה:


Another explanation: **But one sinner destroys a multitude of good.** If Yisroel were half of righteous people and half of wicked ones, and one person came and sinned and made [the wicked] the majority, the result would be that he weighed them all down to be guilty.⁴³

footnotes:
⁴³ Alternatively, when a wise man becomes a sinner, many people will abandon their pursuit of wisdom by concluding that this person’s wisdom was of no avail. (Metsudas Dovid)



## Chapter 10


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:1:1)


**זְבוּבֵי מָוֶת יַבְאִישׁ יַבִּיעַ וְגוֹ'.** כְּגוֹן בִּימֵי הַחֹרֶף, שֶׁאֵין בִּזְבוּבִין כֹּחַ, וְהֵם קְרוֹבִים לָמוּת, גַּם אִם בָּא לְתוֹךְ שֶׁמֶן רוֹקֵחַ וּמִתְעָרֵב בִּבְשָׂמִים, הוּא מַבְאִישׁוֹ, וְהוּא מַעֲלֶה קֶצֶף, שֶׁקּוֹרִין אשקו"א בְלַעַ"ז, וְנִרְאֶה בוֹ כְמִין אֲבַעְבּוּעוֹת, וְזֶהוּ מַשְׁמָעוֹ שֶׁל "יַבִּיעַ". הֲרֵי דָבָר קַל שֶׁהִפְסִיד דָּבָר חָשׁוּב. כַּךְ, יָקָר מֵחָכְמָה וּמִכָּבוֹד הַסִּכְלוּת מְעָט, שֶׁהֲרֵי הִכְרִיעַ אֶת כֻּלָּם. הֲרֵי שֶׁהָיָה אָדָם זֶה שָׁקוּל בְּמֶחֱצָה עֲבֵרוֹת וּמֶחֱצָה זְכֻיּוֹת, וּבָא וְעָבַר עֲבֵרָה אַחַת וְהִכְרִיעַתּוּ לְכַף חוֹבָה, נִמְצֵאת סִכְלוּת זֶה שֶׁהוּא דָבָר מֻעָט, יָקָר וְשָׁקוּל וְכָבֵד יוֹתֵר מִכָּל הַחָכְמָה וְהַכָּבוֹד שֶׁהָיָה בוֹ, שֶׁהֲרֵי הִכְרִיעַ אֶת כֻּלָּם:


**Dead flies decay and pollute, etc.** E.g., in the winter season, flies have no strength, and are near death; nevertheless, if it falls into a perfumer’s oil and becomes mingled with the perfumes, it causes it to become putrid and it assumes a foam, which is called *escume* in O.F., and a sort of bubbles appears in it, and this is the meaning of ‘יַבִּיעַ.’ So an insignificant thing¹ spoiled a precious thing.² So does a little foolishness outweigh wisdom and honor, for it outweighed them all. Suppose that a man was equally divided with transgressions and merits, and he came and committed one transgression, which tipped the scale [causing him] to be guilty. The result is that this foolishness which is small, is heavy and weighs, and is heavier than all the wisdom and honor that he possessed, for behold, it outweighed them all.

footnotes:
¹ I.e., a fly.
² I.e., the perfumer’s oil.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:1:2)


**יָקָר.** לְשׁוֹן כּוֹבֶד. הוּא כָבֵד וְשָׁקוּל יוֹתֵר מִן הַחָכְמָה שֶׁבּוֹ. וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה מוֹשְׁלִים יֵצֶר הָרַע לִזְבוּבֵי מָוֶת:


**[Are] outweighed.** An expression of weight. It is heavy and weighs more than the wisdom that he possesses. The Midrash Aggadah [states], that [Scripture] compares the evil inclination to dying flies.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:1:3)


**יַבְאִישׁ יַבִּיעַ.** שֵׁם טוֹב, שֶׁהוּא עָרֵב מִשֶּׁמֶן רוֹקֵחַ:


**Decay and pollute.** A good name, which is more pleasant than a perfumer’s oil.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:2:1)


**לֵב חָכָם לִימִינוֹ.** חָכְמָתוֹ מְזֻמֶּנֶת לְהַטּוֹתוֹ אֶל דֶּרֶךְ הַמְּיֻמֶּנֶת לְטוֹבָתוֹ:


**The heart [understanding] of a wise man is at his right hand.** His wisdom will turn him onto the proper path for his benefit.³

footnotes:
³ Alternatively, his intellect is always at hand when he needs it. (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:2:2)


**וְלֵב כְּסִיל לִשְׂמֹאלוֹ.** לְעַקְּשׁוֹ מִן הַדֶּרֶךְ הַמְּיֻמֶּנֶת שֶׁהִיא תִפְאֶרֶת וְנוֹחָה לוֹ:


**But the heart of a fool is at his left.** To drive him off the proper path, which is a glory and a comfort for him.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ Alternatively, his intellect is not ready in time of need. (Metsudas Dovid)



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:3:1)


**וְאָמַר לַכֹּל סָכָל הוּא.** בַּהֲלִיכָתוֹ וּבְדִבּוּרוֹ, הַכֹּל מַכִּירִין שֶׁהוּא שׁוֹטֶה:


**And proclaims to all that he is a fool.** By his walk and with his speech, everyone recognizes that he is a fool.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ His actions make it so obvious, that it is as if he proclaims to all that he is a fool. (Ibn Ezra)



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:4:1)


**אִם רוּחַ הַמּוֹשֵׁל.** מוֹשֵׁל הָעוֹלָם תַּעֲלֶה עָלֶיךָ לְדַקְדֵּק אַחֲרֶיךָ בְמִדַּת הַדִּין:


**If the spirit [anger] of the Ruler.** The Ruler of the Universe flares up against you, to scrutinize you with the Divine standard of justice.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:4:2)


**מְקוֹמְךָ אַל תַּנַּח.** מִדָּתְךָ הַטּוֹבָה אַל תַּנַּח, לוֹמַר לוֹ: "מַה יוֹעִיל צִדְקָתִי לִי"?


**Do not leave your place.** Do not set aside your virtuous trait and say to Him, “Of what avail is my righteousness?”


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:4:3)


**כִּי מַרְפֵּא.** דִּקְדּוּקֵי הַדִּין בְּיִסּוּרִין הַבָּאִין עָלֶיךָ, מַרְפֵּא הוּא לַעֲוֹנוֹתֶיךָ, וְיַנִּיחַ לְךָ חֲטָאִים הַגְּדוֹלִים:


**For softness.** The stringencies of the judgment with the afflictions that come upon you are a cure for your sins, and will relieve great sins for you.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:5:1)


**כִּשְׁגָגָה שֶׁיֹּצָא מִלִּפְנֵי הַשַּׁלִּיט.** דּוֹמֶה הוּא לְשַׁלִּיט שֶׁהוֹצִיא דְבַר שְׁגָגָה מִפִּיו שׁוֹגֵג, וְאִי אֶפְשָׁר לַחֲזוֹר, כַּךְ אוֹמֵר הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא, וְאֵין לְהָשִׁיב:


**Like an error emanating from the ruler.** He is like a ruler who unintentionally let an error out of his mouth, and it is irrevocable. So does the Holy One, Blessed Is He, speak, and [His decrees] cannot be retracted.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:6:1)


**נִתַּן הַסֶּכֶל בַּמְּרוֹמִים רַבִּים.** זוּ הִיא הָרָעָה שֶׁהִיא כִּשְׁגָגָה שֶׁיּוֹצָא מִלִּפְנֵי הַשַּׁלִּיט, שֶׁנִּתַּן הַשְּׁטוּת וְהָרֶשַׁע בִּמְרוֹמֵי גֹבַהּ, שֶׁהִגְבִּיהַּ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא, אֶת הַכְּסִילִים וְאֶת הָרְשָׁעִים, שֶׁאֲנִי רוֹאֶה בְרוּחַ הַקֹּדֶשׁ שֶׁהֵם עֲתִידִים לִפְשֹׁט יָד בְּהֵיכָלוֹ וְלָשׂוּם אוֹתוֹתָם אוֹתוֹת:


**Folly is set upon great heights.** This is the evil that is like an error which goes forth from before the ruler, that foolishness and wickedness are placed on the loftiest heights, for the Holy One, Blessed Is He, elevated the fools and the wicked, for I see with the Holy Spirit, that they are destined to extend their hand upon His Temple and to make “their signs for signs.”⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Tehillim 74:4. See also Maseches Gittin 56b.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:6:2)


**וַעֲשִׁירִים בַּשֵּׁפֶל יֵשֵׁבוּ.** יִשְׂרָאֵל, שֶׁכָּל הַגְּדֻלָּה וְהַכָּבוֹד שֶׁיֵּשׁ לָהֶם עַכְשָׁיו בְּיָמַי, עֲתִידִים לֵישֵׁב בַּשֵּׁפֶל, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר, "יֵשְׁבוּ לָאָרֶץ יִדְּמוּ":


**But the rich sit in low places.** Yisroel, for despite all the greatness and honor that they have now in my days,⁷ they are destined to sit in a low place, as it is stated, “they sit on the ground, they are silent.”⁸

footnotes:
⁷ Alternatively, this verse describes the evil that Koheles observes in the previous verse. The rich who are deserving of honor are ignored, while fools and slaves are elevated to high positions that they do not deserve. (Metsudas Dovid)
⁸ Eichah 2:10.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:7:1)


**רָאִיתִי עֲבָדִים עַל סוּסִים.** הֵם כַּשְׂדִּים, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בָּהֶם, "זֶה הָעָם לֹא הָיָה". יִתְעַלּוּ לִהְיוֹת עַל סוּסִים, מוֹלִיכִים אֶת שְׁבוּיֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל אֲסוּרִים בְּקוֹלָרִין:


**I have seen slaves on horses.** These are the Chaldeans, about whom it is stated, “this nation never used to be,”⁹ will be elevated [to ride] on horses, leading Yisroel’s captives, bound with neck irons.

footnotes:
⁹ Yeshayahu 23:13.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:7:2)


**הֹלְכִים כַּעֲבָדִים עַל הָאָרֶץ.** לִפְנֵי קְרוֹנִין שֶׁל כַּשְׂדִּים:


**Walking on foot like slaves on the ground.** Before the Chaldean chariots.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:8:1)


**חֹפֵר גּוּמָּץ.** שׁוּחָה:


**He who digs a pit.** גּוּמָּץ is] a pit.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:8:2)


**בּוֹ יִפּוֹל.** פְּעָמִים שֶׁהוּא נוֹפֵל בּוֹ. כְּלוֹמַר, יֵשׁ לְךָ חוֹרֵשׁ רָעָה וְסוֹפוֹ לָשׁוּב עָלָיו בְּסוֹף, שֶׁכָּלָה זַרְעוֹ שֶׁל נְבוּכַדְנֶאצַּר עַל יְדֵי כְלֵי בֵית הַמִּקְדָּשׁ, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר, "וְעַל מָרֵא שְׁמַיָּא הִתְרוֹמַמְתָּ":


**Will fall into it.** Sometimes he falls into it, i.e., sometimes you have a plotter of evil, and ultimately he is trapped by his own plot, for Nevuchadnetzar’s seed was destroyed through the vessels of the Beis Hamikdosh, as it is stated, “you exalted yourself against the Lord of Heaven.”¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ Daniyeil 5:23. The verse continues, “and the vessels of His House were brought before you, and you, your nobles, your consorts and your concubines drank wine from them, etc.”


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:8:3)


**וּפֹרֵץ גָּדֵר.** סְיָג שֶׁל חֲכָמִים, לַעֲבֹר עַל דִּבְרֵיהֶם:


**He who breaks down a fence.** The fence [established] by the Sages, to transgress their ordinances.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ The Gemara in Maseches Avodah Zarah 27b, applies this verse to those who break down the “fences” established via Rabbinic ordinances which serve to safeguard the Torah; such individuals “will be bitten by a snake,” i.e., death at the hands of Heaven. The destruction caused by such people comes gradually without it being sensed and continuously grows. Its effect is similar to a snake’s venom which continues to spread without realization until it results in death. See Mesilas Yeshorim Chapter 6.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:8:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:8:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:8:4)


**יִשְּׁכֶנּוּ נָחָשׁ.** מִיתָה בִידֵי שָׁמָיִם, וּלְפִי שֶׁדִּבֵּר בִּלְשׁוֹן פְּרִיצַת גָּדֵר, הִזְכִּיר בְּתַשְׁלוּמִין לְשׁוֹן נְשִׁיכַת נָחָשׁ, שֶׁהוּא דָר בְּחוֹרֵי נִקְבֵי כָתְלֵי בָתִּים פְּרוּצִים:


**Will be bitten by a snake.** Death at the hands of Heaven, and because he spoke with an expression of breaking a fence, he mentions his recompense with an expression of the biting of a snake,¹² who nestles in the cracks and holes of the walls of dilapidated houses.

footnotes:
¹² Alternatively, whoever breaks down a fence which acted as a barrier to protect others from being bitten, will himself be bitten. This theme is a repetition of the first phrase, “he who digs, etc,” and continues into the next verse, to stress that these occurrences happen often. (Metsudas Dovid)



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:9:1)


**מַסִיעַ אֲבָנִים יֵעָצֵב בָּהֶם.** מַסִּיעַ אֲבָנִים מִמַּחְצַבְתָּם בֶּהָרִים מִתְיַגֵּעַ בָּהֶם:


**He who removes stones will be hurt by them.** One who quarries stones in the mountains will be fatigued by them.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:9:2)


**יֵעָצֵב.** לְשׁוֹן יְגִיעָה, כְּמוֹ, "בְּעִצָּבוֹן תֹּאכֲלֶנָּה". כְּלוֹמַר, כָּל אָדָם לְפִי מְלַאכְתּוֹ עִצְּבוֹנוֹ, אַף עוֹשֵׂה רָעָה, לְפִי זְרִיעָתוֹ יִקְצוֹר:


**Will be hurt.** ‘יֵעָצֵב’ is an expression of weariness, as in, “with toil בְּעִצָּבוֹן shall you eat it,”¹³ i.e., every man, his weariness is according to his work, so will the doer of evil reap according to what he sows.

footnotes:
¹³ Bereishis 3:17. Alternatively, whoever scatters rocks to trip others, will himself be tripped and hurt by them. (Alshich)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:9:3)


**יִסָּכֶן בָּם.** יִתְחַמֵּם בָּהֶם, כְּמוֹ "וּתְהִי לוֹ סֹכֶנֶת". אַף הָעוֹסֵק בַּתּוֹרָה וּבְמִצְוֹת, סוֹפוֹ לֵהָנוֹת מֵהֶם:


**Will be endangered by them.** Shall be warmed by them, as in, “and she shall be to him a warmer סֹכֶנֶת.”¹⁴ So will one who engages in [the study of] Torah and in [the performance of] commandments ultimately benefit from them.

footnotes:
¹⁴ I Melochim 1:2.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:10:1)


**אִם קֵהָה הַבַּרְזֶל.** חַרְבוֹת צוּרִים, שֶׁקֵּהָה פִּיהֶם וְחִדּוּדָם:


**If the axe is blunt.** Sharp swords, whose sharp edges have become dull.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁵ The Gemara in Maseches Ta’anis 7b, applies this verse to the type of student to whom studies are hard as iron [=ברזל]. It attributes the reason for this problem is that he did not organize his studies properly [= והוא לא פנים קלקל]. His remedy is to exert more energy in his studies [=וחילים יגבר] and to spend more time in Yeshiva [=ויתרון הכשר חכמה].


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:10:2)


**וְהוּא לֹא פָנִים קִלְקַל.** וְאֵינָם לְטוּשִׁים וּמְרוּטִים: "לְמַעַן הֱיֵה לָה בָּרָק." אַף עַל פִּי כֵן "וַחֲיָלִים יְגַבֵּר". מַגְבִּיר הוּא בְמִלְחָמָה אֶת גִּבּוֹרֵי הַחֲיָלִים לְנַצֵּחַ:


**And one has not sharpened its edge.** And they are not sharpened and burnished,¹⁶ [as in,] “in order that they may glitter.”¹⁷ Nonetheless, it still strengthens the armies. It strengthens the mighty men of the armies in battle to be victorious.

footnotes:
¹⁶ קלקל is a double form of קלל, as in נחשת קלל [=burnished brass], in Yechezkeil 1:7. (Ibn Ezra)
¹⁷ Yechezkeil 21:15. The text in our editions reads למען היה לה ברק [=in order that it may glitter].


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:10:3)


**וְיִתְרוֹן הַכְשֵׁיר חָכְמָה.** וּמַעֲלַת כִּשְׁרוֹן יֵשׁ עוֹד לַחָכְמָה יוֹתֵר מִן הַבַּרְזֶל. אִם תַּלְמִיד חָכָם מַשְׁחִיר פָּנָיו בְּרָעָב, וְאַתָּה רוֹאֵהוּ מִסְכֵּן בֵּין הָעֲשִׁירִים, הַרְבֵּה חֲיָלִים מִתְגַּבְּרִים עַל יָדוֹ. וְאַל תִּתְמַהּ עַל וָי"ו "וַחֲיָלִים", כִּי הַרְבֵּה וָוִי"ן נוֹפְלִים כֵּן בְּלָשׁוֹן עִבְרִית, כְּמוֹ "אִם רָאִיתָ גַנָּב וַתִּרֶץ עִמּוֹ": "עָזִּי וְזִמְרָת יָהּ וַיְהִי לִי לִישׁוּעָה", וְהַרְבֵּה מְפֹרָשִׁים כָּזֶה:


**There is more profit in a skill of wisdom.** Wisdom has an additional advantage over iron, for if a Torah scholar’s face is blackened by hunger, and you see him as a poor man among the rich, [realize that] many armies are strengthened by him. Do not wonder about the *vav* of ‘וַחֲיָלִים,’ for many *vavin* appear like that in the Hebrew language, as in, “If you saw a thief, you agreed וַתִּרֶץ with him,”¹⁸ [and as in,] “The strength and the retribution of God was וַיְהִי to me a salvation,”¹⁹. and many [others] are similarly explained.

footnotes:
¹⁸ Tehillim 50:18.
¹⁹ Shemos 15:2. Rashi there gives numerous examples of Scripture’s similar usage of the vav



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:11:1)


**אִם יִשֹּׁךְ הַנָּחָשׁ.** אֶת אִישׁ:


**If the snake bites.** A person.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:11:2)


**בְּלוֹא לָחַשׁ.** מֵחֲמַת שֶׁלֹּא לָחְשׁוֹ הֶחָבֵר שֶׁלֹּא יִשֹּׁךְ:


**Before it is charmed.** Because the charmer did not charm it not to bite.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:11:3)


**וְאֵין יִתְרוֹן.** לֶחָבֵר הָרָשָׁע שֶׁהָיָה רָגִיל לְלָחְשׁוֹ, בְּלֹא לָחַשׁ, כַּךְ, אִם בְנֵי עִירְךָ נִכְשָׁלִים בְּאִסּוּרִין מֵחֲמַת שֶׁאֵין חָכָם דּוֹרֵשׁ לָהֶם וּמְלַמְּדָם אֶת חֻקֵּי הַתּוֹרָה, אֵין יִתְרוֹן לוֹ בִשְׁתִיקָתוֹ, וְלֹא יִשְׂתַּכֵר:


**There is no advantage.** To the wicked charmer who was accustomed to charm it, if he did not charm it. So, if the people of your city [are unaware and] stumble over prohibitions because the wise man does not preach to them or teach them the statutes of the Torah, he has no advantage with his silence, and he will not benefit.²⁰ ²¹

footnotes:
²⁰ Alternatively, Koheles is comparing a slanderer to a charmer. A person who allows his tongue to slander is as guilty as a charmer whose snake killed someone because he did not prevent it from doing so. One’s slander often causes havoc and destruction, and sometimes even death. (Alshich)
²¹ See the Gemara in Maseches Arachin 15b, which applies this verse to a slanderer [=בעל לשון הרע].



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:12:1)


**דִּבְרֵי פִי חָכָם חֵן.** עַל שׁוֹמְעֵיהֶם וְנִשְׁמָעִים לוֹ, וְטוֹב לוֹ שֶׁנּוֹטֵל גְּדֻלָּה עֲלֵיהֶם:


**The words of a wise man find favor.** In the eyes of their listeners, and they listen to him, and it is good for him that he assumes greatness over them.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:12:2)


**וְשִׁפְתוֹת כְּסִיל תְּבַלְּעֶנּוּ.** זֶה הַמֵּסִית אֶת חֲבֵרוֹ מִדֶּרֶךְ טוֹבָה, כְּגוֹן בִּלְעָם, שֶׁפָּרַץ גְּדֵרָן שֶׁל עַכּוּ"ם שֶׁגָּדְרוּ עַצְמָן מִן הָעֲרָיוֹת מִדּוֹר הַמַּבּוּל וְאֵילֵךְ, וְהוּא יָעַץ לָהֶם לִהְיוֹת מַפְקִירִין בְּנוֹתֵיהֶם לִזְנוּת:


**But lips of a fool devour him.** This refers to someone who misleads his fellow man from the good way [to the bad way]; e.g., Bilam, who broke the restrictions that the nations had imposed, that they restricted themselves from immorality from the time of the generation of the flood and onwards, yet, he advised them to offer their daughters for prostitution.²²

footnotes:
²² Alternatively, Targum renders, “the words of a wise man find favor,” i.e., by his prayer he finds favor before God, Who removes the retribution destined to befall the world; “but the lips of a fool,” are words of reproach and bring destruction to the world.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:13:1)


**תְּחִלַּת דִּבְרֵי פִיהוּ סִכְלוּת.** כְּשֶׁאָמַר לוֹ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא: "מִי הָאֲנָשִׁים הָאֵלֶּה עִמָּךְ"? הָיָה לוֹ לְהָשִׁיב "ה', אַתָּה יוֹדֵעַ" וְהוּא אָמַר "בָּלָק בֶּן צִפּוֹר מֶלֶךְ מוֹאָב שָׁלַח אֵלָי". כְּלוֹמַר: אִם בְּעֵינֶיךָ אֲנִי נִבְזֶה, חָשׁוּב אֲנִי בְעֵינֵי מַלְכֵי הָאָרֶץ:


**The beginning of his words is foolishness.** When the Holy One, Blessed Is He, said to him [Bilam], “Who are these men with you?”²³ he should have replied, “Adonoy, You know,” but [instead] he said, “Balak son of Tzippor, king of Mo’av sent them to me,”²⁴ i.e., though I may be despised in Your eyes, I am esteemed in the eyes of the earthly kings.

footnotes:
²³ Bamidbar 22:9.
²⁴ Ibid., verse 10.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:13:2)


**וְאַחֲרִית פִּיהוּ הוֹלֵלוּת.** שִׁעְמוּם וְעִרְבּוּב: "לְכָה אִיעָצְךָ" זִמַּת אִוֶּלֶת:


**And the end of talk is [evil] madness.** Madness and confusion [by advising], “Come I will counsel you” with foolish lewdness.²⁵

footnotes:
²⁵ Alternatively, from beginning [=תחלת] to end [=ואחרית], a fool’s statements are folly and madness. (Metsudas Dovid)



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:14:1)


**וְהַסָּכָל יַרְבֶּה דְּבָרִים.** שֶׁהָיָה מִתְפָּאֵר בְּעַצְמוֹ שֶׁהָיָה יוֹדֵעַ דַּעַת עֶלְיוֹן:


**The fool multiplies words.** For he [Bilam] was boasting that he, “knows the knowledge of the Most High.”²⁶

footnotes:
²⁶ Bamidbar 24:16.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:14:2)


**לֹא יֵדַע הָאָדָם מַה שֶׁיִּהְיֶה.** לוֹ לְאַחַר זְמַן. שֶׁהֲרֵי הָלַךְ לִיטֹּל שְׂכָרוֹ בְמִדְיָן, וְלֹא יָדַע שֶׁיִּפּוֹל בֶּחָרֶב.


**Man knows not what will be.** To him in the future, for he went to take his pay in Midyan, but he did not know that he would be killed by the sword.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:14:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:14:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:14:3)


וּלְפִי פְּשׁוּטוֹ: **הַסָּכָל יַרְבֶּה דְבָרִים.** גּוֹזֵר וְאוֹמֵר: "מָחָר אֶעֱשֶׂה כֵן לִפְלוֹנִי", וְאֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ מַה יִּהְיֶה מָחָר:


But according to its simple meaning: **The fool multiplies words.** He declares and says, “Tomorrow I will do this to so-and-so,” without knowing what will be tomorrow.²⁷

footnotes:
²⁷ Alternatively, although the fool multiplies his words, the listener nonetheless knows neither their meaning nor his intention. (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:14:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:14:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:14:4)


**וַאֲשֶׁר יִהְיֶה מֵאַחֲרָיו, מִי יַגִּיד לוֹ.** כְּלוֹמַר: לֹא סוֹף דָּבָר שֶׁאֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ מַה יִּהְיֶה לְאַחַר זְמַן, אֶלָּא אַף הַהֹוֶה עַכְשָׁיו מֵאַחֲרָיו, שֶׁלֹּא כְנֶגֶד עֵינָיו אֶלָּא מֵאֲחוֹרֵי עָרְפּוֹ מֵרָחוֹק, צָרִיךְ הוּא שֶׁיְּהֵא מִי יַגִּיד לוֹ:


**And what will be after him who can tell him.** I.e., not only does he not know what will be after a time, but even what is presently now behind him, but is not in front of his eyes, only behind his back at a distance, he needs someone to tell him.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:15:1)


**עֲמַל הַכְּסִילִים תְּיַגְּעֶנּוּ.** כְּסִילוּתָם גּוֹרֵם לָהֶם עָמָל הַמְיַגְּעָן, אֲשֶׁר לֹא לָמְדוּ דַרְכֵי מְבוֹאוֹת הָעִיר וּמִתְיַגֵּעַ לִכְנוֹס דֶּרֶךְ פְּתָחִים וּבִצְעֵי הַמַּיִם, וְיָגֵעַ בִּטְבִיעוּת רַגְלָיו בַּבּוּץ. כְּלוֹמַר: עַצְלוּת שֶׁל עוֹזְבֵי הַתּוֹרָה גוֹרֵם לָהֶם יְגִיעַת עָמָל בְּגֵיהִנֹּם:


**The labor of the fools exhausts them.** Their foolishness causes them labor,²⁸ which wearies them, because they did not learn the ways of the approaches to the city, and he becomes weary by entering by way of pits and marshes, and he is wearied by his feet sinking into the mire; i.e., the laziness of those who abandon the Torah causes them wearying labor in Gehinnom.

footnotes:
²⁸ Fools busy themselves to understand profound and mysterious matters, when they lack comprehension of even simple matters. They are compared to those who wish to enter a city but do not know the way. They stumble and grow weary without achieving their goal. (Ibn Ezra)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:15:2)


**אֲשֶׁר לֹא יָדַע לָלֶכֶת.** אֶל נְתִיב אֱמֶת לִפְרוֹשׁ מִן הָעֲבֵרָה, לְפִי שֶׁלֹּא לָמַד תּוֹרָה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר, "נֵר לְרַגְלִי דְבָרֶיךָ":


**For he does not know the way.** To the path of truth, to part from transgression,²⁹ because he did not study Torah, as it is stated, “Your word is a lamp for my feet.”³⁰

footnotes:
²⁹ Alternatively, this phrase refers to an ignorant person who does not go to the city and learn from its wise people. (Targum)
³⁰ Tehillim 119:105.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:16:1)


**אִי לָךְ אֶרֶץ שֶׁמַּלְכֵּךְ.** בִּזְמַן שֶׁמַּלְכֵּךְ וְדַּיָּנַיִךְ מִתְנַהֲגִים בְּנַעֲרוּת:


**Woe to you, O land if your king.** When your king and your judges behave childishly.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:17:1)


**בִּגְבוּרָה וְלֹא בַשְּׁתִי.** שֶׁעוֹסְקִין בִּגְבוּרַת הַחָכְמָה וְהַבִּינָה, וְלֹא בִשְׁתִיַּת יָיִן:


**In strength and not in drunkenness.** They engage in the power of wisdom and understanding and not in drinking wine.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:18:1)


**בַּעֲצַלְתַּיִם יִמַּךְ הַמְּקָרֶה.** כְּשֶׁאָדָם מִתְעַצֵּל וְאֵינוֹ מְתַקֵּן הַפְּרִיצָה קְטַנָּה שֶׁבְּתִקְרַת הַבָּיִת:


**Where there is slothfulness the ceiling sags.** When a person is lazy and does not repair a small breach in the ceiling of the house.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:18:2)


**יִמַּךְ הַמְּקָרֶה.** יִשְׁפַּל הַבִּנְיָן הַמְּקָרֶה אֶת הַבַּיִת וְהַמְּסַכֵּךְ עָלָיו:


**The ceiling sags.** The structure and beams of the house will sag— and its covering.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:18:3)


**יִדְלֹף.** יְטַפְטֵף דֶּלֶךְ גְּשָׁמִים, כְּלוֹמַר: כְּשֶׁיִּשְׂרָאֵל מִתְעַצְּלִים בַּתּוֹרָה, הֵם נְמַקִּים, וּבֵית גְּאוֹן עֻזָּם חָרֵב וּמָךְ:


**Leaks.** The dripping rain leaks, i.e., when [Bnei] Yisroel are lazy with [observance of] the Torah,³¹ they melt away, and the Beis [Hamikdosh], the pride of their strength, becomes destroyed and impoverished.

footnotes:
³¹ See Maseches Ta’anis 7b.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:19:1)


**לִשְׂחוֹק עֹשִׂים לֶחֶם.** לְחֶדְוַת מִזְמוּטֵי חֲתָנִים וְכַלּוֹת, עוֹשִׂים סְעוּדָה, וּסְתָם סְעוּדָה גְדוֹלָה קְרוּיָה לֶחֶם, כְּמוֹ דְאַתְּ אָמַר: "בֵּלְשַׁצַּר מַלְכָּא עֲבַד לֶחֶם רַב":


**A feast is made for laughter.** For the joy of the musical entertainment at a wedding, we make a feast, and an ordinary large feast is called ‘לֶחֶם,’ as it is stated, “King Belshatzar made a great feast לֶחֶם.”³²

footnotes:
³² Daniyeil 5:1.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:19:2)


**וְיַיִן.** מַשְׁקִין בִּסְעוּדָה, אֲשֶׁר יְשַׂמַּח הַחַיִּים:


**And wine.** They give to drink at the feast, that which makes life joyful.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:19:3)


**וְהַכֶּסֶף יַעֲנֶה אֶת הַכֹּל.** אִם אֵין כֶּסֶף, אֵין סְעוּדָה. לְפִיכָךְ, לֹא יִתְעַצֵּל אָדָם מִן הַמְלָאכָה, כְּדֵי שֶׁיְּהֵא לוֹ מַה לְהוֹצִיא:


**And money answers all things.** Without money, there is no feast; therefore, a person should not neglect work, so that he will have what to spend.³³

footnotes:
³³ Alternatively, “money afflicts [=יענה] all,” i.e., either the abundance or lack of money causes affliction to mankind. Or, “money makes everyone respond [=יענה],” i.e., money causes people to readily listen and to obey.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:20:1)


**גַּם בְּמַדָּעֲךָ מֶלֶךְ.** אֲפִילּוּ בְמַחְשְׁבוֹתֶיךָ, בְּלֹא דִבּוּר. "גַּם" אֲפִילוּ.


**Even in your thoughts [do not curse] the king.** Even in your thoughts, without speech. “גַּם” means “even.”


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:20:2)


**מֶלֶךְ אַל תְּקַלֵּל.** אַל תַּרְגִּיז לְמַלְכּוֹ שֶׁל עוֹלָם. דָּבָר אַחֵר: כְּמַשְׁמָעוֹ, מֶלֶךְ בָּשָׂר וָדָם:


**Do not curse the king.** Do not provoke the King of the Universe. Another explanation: According to its apparent meaning, this is a mortal king.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:20:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:20:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:20:3)


**עוֹף הַשָּׁמַיִם.** נְשָׁמָה הַנְּתוּנָה בְךָ, שֶׁסּוֹפָה לָעוּף עַל הַשָּׁמָיִם:


**A bird of the skies.** The soul, which is placed within you, which will ultimately fly up to the heaven.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 10:20:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:20:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/10:20:4)


**וּבַעַל כְּנָפַיִם.** מַלְאָךְ הַמְלַוֶּה אוֹתְךָ, כְּעִנְיָן שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר: "כִּי מַלְאָכָיו יְצַוֶּה לָךְ", וּלְפִי מַשְׁמָעוֹ, הָעוֹבְרִים וְהַשָּׁבִים. יֵשׁ לְךָ לִדְאֹג מִכָּל בִּרְיָה, שֶׁמָּא יֵשׁ שׁוֹמְעִין וְיַגִּידוּ לַאֲחֵרִים:


**And that which has wings.** The angel who escorts you, as the matter is stated, “For He will command His angels on your behalf,”³⁴ and according to its apparent meaning, “those who go back and forth;” [i.e.,] you should worry about every person, perhaps there are listeners, who will tell [the matter] to others.³⁵

footnotes:
³⁴ Tehillim 91:11.
³⁵ The Gemara in Maseches Bava Basra 4a states, that when Bava the son of Butta met with Hordos he applied this verse.



## Chapter 11


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 11:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:1:1)


**שַׁלַּח לַחְמְךָ עַל פְּנֵי הַמָּיִם.** עֲשֵׂה טוֹבָה וָחֶסֶד לְאָדָם שֶׁיֹּאמַר לְךָ לִבְּךָ עָלָיו, "אַל תִּרְאֶנּוּ עוֹד", כְּאָדָם שֶׁמַּשְׁלִיךְ מְזוֹנוֹתָיו עַל פְּנֵי הַמָּיִם:


**Cast you your bread upon the waters.** Do goodness and kindness to a person about whom your heart tells you that you will never see him again,¹ like a person who casts his food upon the water’s surface.²

footnotes:
¹ Do kindness without anticipating reciprocity from the receiver. (Sforno) Or, when giving charity, needy Torah scholars should be given priority. The Gemara in Maseches Bava Kamma 17a states that Torah is symbolized by water. Thus, when you “cast your bread,” cast it first to the needy Torah Scholars. (Derech Hachayim)
² Alternatively, also give charity to people you do not know. (Ibn Ezra)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 11:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:1:2)


**כִּי בְרֹב הַיָּמִים תִּמְצָאֶנּוּ.** עוֹד יָמִים בָּאִים וּתְקַבֵּל תַּשְׁלוּמְךָ. רְאֵה מַה נֶאֱמַר בְּיִתְרוֹ: "קִרְאֶן לוֹ וְיֹאכַל לָחֶם". וְסָבוּר שֶׁהוּא מִצְרִי, וְלֹא יִרְאֶנּוּ עוֹד. מֶה הָיָה סוֹפוֹ? נַעֲשָׂה חֲתָנוֹ וּמָלַךְ עַל יִשְׂרָאֵל, וְהִכְנִיסוֹ תַחַת כַּנְפֵי הַשְּׁכִינָה, וְזָכוּ בָנָיו וּבְנֵי בָנָיו לֵישֵׁב בְּלִשְׁכַּת הַגָּזִית:


**For after many days you will find it.** Days will yet come, and you will receive your recompense.³ Look what is stated about Yisro, “Call him and let him eat bread,”⁴ and he thought that he [Moshe] was an Egyptian and that he would never see him again. What was the end? He became his son-in-law and reigned over Yisroel and [Moshe] brought him under the wings of the Divine Presence, and his sons and grandsons merited to sit in the Chamber of Hewn Stone.

footnotes:
³ Alternatively, for you will find its reward in the World to Come. (Targum)
⁴ Shemos 2:20.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 11:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:2:1)


**תֶּן חֵלֶק לְשִׁבְעָה וְגַם לִשְׁמוֹנָה.** חִלַּקְתָּ מִלַּחְמְךָ וּמִשְׁתְּךָ לְשִׁבְעָה צְרִיכֵי חֶסֶד, חַלֵּק עוֹד לִשְׁמוֹנָה שֶׁיָּבֹאוּ אַחֲרֵיהֶם, וְאַל תֹּאמַר "דָּי":


**Contribute to seven and even eight.** If you shared your food and your drink with seven who need kindness,⁵ share further with eight more who come after them, and do not say, “Enough.”⁶

footnotes:
⁵ Alternatively, when giving charity, give priority to the needy who are Shabbos observers [=לשבעה], symbolized by the seventh day of the week, Shabbos, over the needy who do not observe Shabbos; and give priority to Jews [=לשבעה], symbolized by the eighth day of circumcision], over non-Jews. (Derech Hachayim)
⁶ The Rambam lists eight levels of charity, each higher than the other. The seventh [i.e., next to the highest level] is a person who gives charity to one whose identity he does not know, nor does the recipient know the identity of the donor. The highest [i.e., eighth] level, is supporting one by means of a gift, loan, partnership of providing work to an impoverished Jew. Alternatively, the verse means, “contribute on the seventh and eighth level [as prescribed by the Rambam].” (Alshich)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 11:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:2:2)


**כִּי לֹא תֵדַע מַה יִהְיֶה רָעָה.** שֶׁמָּא עוֹד בָּאִים וְתִצְטָרֵךְ לְכֻלָּם. אָז תִּנָּצֵל עַל יְדֵי צְדָקָה זוּ מִן הָרָעָה. וְאִם לֹא עַכְשָׁיו, אֵימָתָי?


**For you never know what calamity will come.** Perhaps days will yet come and you will need [support from] them all. Then you will be saved from the evil by this charity, and if not now, when?


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 11:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:2:3)


וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ: **תֶּן חֵלֶק וְגוֹ'.** אֵלּוּ שִׁבְעַת יְמֵי בְרֵאשִׁית. תֵּן אֶחָד מֵהֶן חֵלֶק לְיוֹצְרֶךָ לָנוּחַ בְּשַׁבָּת. **וְגַם לִשְׁמוֹנָה.** אֵלּוּ שְׁמוֹנַת יְמֵי הַמִּילָהּ.


Our Rabbis, however, said—**Contribute to, etc.** These are the seven days of creation. Give one of them as a portion to your Creator, by resting on Shabbos. **And even to eight.** These are the eight days preceding circumcision.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 11:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:2:4)


דָּבָר אַחֵר: **תֶּן חֵלֶק לְשִׁבְעָה.** קָרְבְּנוֹת צִבּוּר שֶׁל שִׁבְעַת יְמֵי פֶסַח. **וְגַם לִשְׁמוֹנָה.** שֶׁל שְׁמוֹנַת יְמֵי הֶחָג:


Another explanation: **Contribute to seven.** The communal sacrifices of the seven days of Pesach. **And even to eight.** The eight days of the Festival [=Succos].


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 11:2:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:2:5)


**כִּי לֹא תֵדַע מַה יִהְיֶה רָעָה.** אִם יֶחֱרַב הַבַּיִת, וְלֹא תַקְרִיבוּ עוֹד, וְיוֹעִילוּ קָרְבָּנוֹת הָרִאשׁוֹנִים, לְבַטֵּל גְּזֵרָה הָרָעָה:


**For you never know what calamity will come.** If the Beis [Hamikdosh] will be destroyed, and you will no longer bring sacrifices, and the first sacrifices will avail to annul the evil decree.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 11:2:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:2:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:2:6)


דָבָר אַחֵר: **כִּי לֹא תֵדַע מַה יִהְיֶה רָעָה.** לֹא יָדַעְתָּ מַה נִגְזַר עַל הַגְּשָׁמִים בַּחַג. וְיוֹעִילוּ הַקָּרְבָּנוֹת, וִיבַטְּלוּ גְזֵרוֹת רָעוֹת:


Another explanation: **For you never know what [calamity] will come.** You do not know what has been decreed on the Festival regarding the rains, and the sacrifices will avail to annul evil decrees.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 11:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:3:1)


**אִם יִמָּלְאוּ הֶעָבִים גֶּשֶׁם.** אִם רָאִיתָ עָבִים מְלֵאִים גֶּשֶׁם, יָדַעְתָּ שֶׁסּוֹפָן יָרִיקוּ גִשְׁמֵיהֶם עַל הָאָרֶץ; בַּמָּקוֹם שֶׁהַטּוֹבָה צוֹמַחַת וְנִכֶּרֶת, שָׁם סוֹפָהּ לָנוּחַ. אַף כֵּן דַּע שֶׁאִם יִפּוֹל עֵץ וְגוֹ', אִם יִשְׁכֹּן אָדָם חָכָם וְצַדִּיק בְּעִיר אוֹ בִמְדִינָה, מְקוֹם שֶׁיִּשְׁכֹּן, שָׁם יִהְיוּ נִכָּרִין מַעֲשָׂיו אַחֲרֵי מוֹתוֹ, וְחָכְמוֹתָיו וּמִדּוֹת תְּרוּמִיּוֹתָיו וְתַשְׁלוּם טוֹבָה לְיוֹשְׁבֵי הַמָּקוֹם עַל מִנְהָגוֹ הַטּוֹב שֶׁהִדְרִיכָם בְּדֶּרֶךְ יְשָׁרָה:


**When the clouds are filled with rain.** If you have seen clouds full of rain, you know that they will ultimately empty their rains upon the earth. In the place where the benefit grows and is discernible, there it is destined [for the rains] to come to rest. Likewise, you should know that “if a tree falls, etc.,” [i.e.,] if a wise and righteous man⁷ resides in a city or in a province, the place where he resides, there his deeds will be discernible after his death, and his words of wisdom, exemplary traits and bestowal of goodness upon the inhabitants of that place by his good custom that he guided them on a straight road.⁸

footnotes:
⁷ In Bamidbar 13:20, Rashi explains that a tree [עץ] is symbolizes a righteous man. See also the last Rashi on this verse below.
⁸ Alternatively, this verse is connected to the previous verse, i.e., a person blessed with wealth must share his fortune with the less fortunate in the same manner as clouds that are full of rain do not keep it for themselves but empty their rains upon the earth. (Metsudas Dovid)


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 11:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:3:2)


**יִפּוֹל.** יִשְׁכֹּן, כְּמוֹ "עַל פְּנֵי כָל אֶחָיו נָפָל":


**Falls.** Will reside, as in, “over all his brothers did he reside נָפָל.”⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Bereishis 25:18.


###### Rashi on Ecclesiastes 11:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Ecclesiastes/r/11:3:3)


**עֵץ.** תַּלְמִיד חָכָם שֶׁמֵּגִין בִּזְכוּתוֹ כָּעֵץ הַמֵּסִיךְ עַל הָאָרֶץ:


**A tree.** [Symbolic of] a Torah scholar, who with his merit protects like a tree, which serves as a covering over the earth.



### Verse 4
